Contrast baths are reserved for later stages when swelling is stable, and icing for 45 minutes with the foot hanging down risks tissue cold injury and gravity-driven edema.
  19. The correct answer is B (Lysine, which human cells cannot synthesize and which must therefore come from dietary protein): Essential amino acids are those humans cannot make and must obtain from food, and lysine is one of them. Tyrosine is made from phenylalanine by phenylalanine hydroxylase, so it is not strictly essential if phenylalanine is adequate. Cysteine can be made from methionine and serine, and glutamate is formed from alpha-ketoglutarate by transamination. Keeping essential and nonessential amino acids straight is the point of this classification.
  20. The correct answer is D (Traumatic spondylolisthesis of C2, produced by hyperextension combined with axial loading of the neck): A forehead impact drives the head into hyperextension and compresses the neck, fracturing the C2 pars interarticularis on both sides and letting the C2 body slip forward, called a hangman fracture. The prevertebral soft tissues swell in front of C2. A Jefferson fracture is a C1 ring burst, a clay shoveler fracture is at C7, and a dens fracture runs through the odontoid process, not the pars.
  21. The correct answer is C (Inferior wall infarction, usually from the right coronary artery, so activate emergency services for immediate transfer): Leads II, III and aVF look at the inferior wall of the left ventricle, which is supplied by the right coronary artery in most people, and reciprocal depression in I and aVL supports a true occlusion. ST elevation with ongoing chest pressure is a STEMI, a time-critical emergency needing immediate EMS activation for reperfusion. Anterior infarction would raise V1 to V4, lateral involvement would raise I, aVL, V5 and V6, and pericarditis shows diffuse concave elevation without reciprocal change except in aVR.
  22. The correct answer is A (Probable benign prostatic hyperplasia; refer for physician evaluation and co-manage musculoskeletal care): Slowly progressive hesitancy, weak stream and nocturia in an older man without red flags fit benign prostatic hyperplasia, which a physician should evaluate while any musculoskeletal care is co-managed. Cauda equina syndrome brings saddle anesthesia, leg weakness and retention with sudden onset. Metastatic disease would usually bring bone pain or weight loss. A facet joint does not cause obstructive voiding symptoms.
  23. The correct answer is B (Immunity from vaccination, because surface antibody is present while core antibody is absent from the serum): Vaccine contains only recombinant surface antigen, so vaccination produces antibody to the surface antigen alone. Natural infection also produces antibody to the core antigen, so a negative core antibody with a positive surface antibody means vaccine immunity. A recovered patient would be positive for both antibodies. Chronic infection shows persistent surface antigen, and the window period lacks detectable surface antibody, which does not fit a level of 150 mIU/mL.
  24. The correct answer is A (C8 and T1 roots forming the lower trunk of the brachial plexus): Excessive traction on an abducted arm stretches the lower trunk, C8 and T1, which supplies the small muscles of the hand. The result is a claw hand with weak intrinsic muscles. T1 also carries preganglionic sympathetic fibers, so a Horner syndrome can accompany it. Upper trunk injury affects the shoulder and elbow instead.
  25. The correct answer is A (The lumbar region, where bodies are large and costal facets are absent): A kidney-shaped body, a short squared spinous process that projects horizontally backward and the absence of costal facets are characteristic of lumbar vertebrae. Lower thoracic vertebrae still carry costal facets on the body. Sacral segments fuse, and cervical vertebrae have a small body and transverse foramina.
  26. The correct answer is B (Ryanodine receptor of the sarcoplasmic reticulum releasing Ca2+ uncontrollably, treated with dantrolene): Malignant hyperthermia results from a ryanodine receptor defect, triggered by volatile anesthetics or succinylcholine, causing uncontrolled Ca2+ release from the sarcoplasmic reticulum. Sustained contraction produces heat, rigidity and CO2. Dantrolene blocks the ryanodine receptor, whereas verapamil, bromocriptine and neostigmine do not act on that channel.
  27. The correct answer is D (Osteonecrosis of the femoral head, caused by ischemia related to chronic alcohol use): Groin pain in an alcohol user with a subchondral lucent crescent, patchy sclerosis, and a preserved joint space fits avascular necrosis, because alcohol and steroids impair femoral head blood supply. Early on the joint space is normal, and later the head flattens and collapses. Transient osteoporosis is osteopenic without sclerosis, osteoarthritis narrows the joint with osteophytes, and a stress fracture shows a line across the neck.
  28. The correct answer is A (L4 root, supplying the medial leg, medial malleolus and tibialis anterior): The medial malleolus and medial leg are the L4 dermatome, and tibialis anterior is the main L4 dorsiflexor. The patellar reflex is mainly L4 as well, so a diminished reflex fits. L5 covers the dorsal foot and great toe, S1 covers the lateral foot and the Achilles reflex, and L3 covers the thigh and knee. One dermatome, one myotome and one reflex all point to a single root.
  29. The correct answer is D (Chronic arteriolar sclerosis from sustained pressure, reflecting long standing end organ vascular damage): Arteriovenous nicking and copper wiring are signs of chronic hypertensive arteriolar thickening and sclerosis. They mark long exposure to high pressure and suggest similar vascular change in the kidney and brain. Papilledema, flame hemorrhages and cotton wool spots would define acute malignant disease, none of which are present. Neovascularization belongs to diabetes, and an artery occlusion causes sudden painless visual loss with a pale retina.
  30. The correct answer is C (Parietal cells of the body secrete hydrochloric acid and intrinsic factor into the lumen): Parietal cells in the gastric body secrete hydrochloric acid and intrinsic factor, which is needed for vitamin B12 absorption. Chief cells secrete pepsinogen, which acid converts to pepsin. G cells in the antrum release gastrin, and mucous neck cells secrete mucus and a bicarbonate-rich fluid, not acid or gastrin.
  31. The correct answer is C (Iron deficiency, since ferritin stores are depleted and the liver raises transferrin to capture iron): Ferritin is the storage form of iron, so a ferritin of 6 ng/mL shows depleted stores. Transferrin carries iron in plasma, and its capacity (TIBC) rises when iron is scarce. Low serum iron with high TIBC and a low MCV is the classic pattern in iron deficiency from chronic blood loss. Chronic disease gives low TIBC and high ferritin, thalassemia trait has normal iron studies, and hemochromatosis shows iron overload.
  32. The correct answer is A (Sagittal plane, about a transverse axis running side to side): Flexion and extension move a segment forward or backward, so they occur in the sagittal plane. The axis is always perpendicular to the plane, which means a transverse axis running side to side. Lateral bending belongs to the frontal plane about an anteroposterior axis, and axial rotation belongs to the transverse plane about a longitudinal axis.
  33. The correct answer is C (Send him to the emergency department now for urgent MRI and surgical evaluation, without any manipulation): Saddle anesthesia, urinary retention, reduced sphincter tone, and bilateral leg weakness indicate cauda equina syndrome, a surgical emergency. Outcomes depend on decompression within roughly 24 to 48 hours, so emergency transfer is required. Manipulation or traction is contraindicated, and waiting a week or arranging elective care risks permanent bladder and bowel dysfunction.
  34. The correct answer is B (Rotation of the vertebral body to the right, with the spinous process swinging left): In the lower cervical spine the facets lie about 45 degrees from horizontal, so lateral flexion is coupled with rotation to the same side. The vertebral body rotates toward the side of flexion while the spinous process moves the opposite way. Lumbar coupling is the reverse pattern for the body in neutral posture. Uncinate processes limit lateral translation but do not drive rotation.
  35. The correct answer is C (Grade II, since the slip is between 25 and 50 percent of the sacral base): Meyerding divides the sacral base into quarters, so a slip of 0 to 25 percent is grade I, 25 to 50 percent is grade II, 50 to 75 percent is grade III, and over 75 percent is grade IV. A 40 percent slip is therefore grade II. The reference is the sacral base width, not the L5 body. Grade II in a young athlete is usually managed conservatively with follow up films.
  36. The correct answer is A (Enchondroma, with stippled chondroid calcification in rings and arcs inside a lytic lesion): An incidental, well marginated lytic lesion in the proximal humerus with rings and arcs calcification is an enchondroma, a benign cartilage tumor. The matrix pattern is the key feature, and no cortical breach or periosteal reaction argues against malignancy. Fibrous dysplasia shows ground glass density, osteosarcoma gives aggressive new bone, and a bone infarct has a serpentine border.
  37. The correct answer is C (Recognize probable diabetic ketoacidosis and arrange immediate ambulance transport to a hospital): Vomiting, fruity breath, deep rapid Kussmaul breathing, tachycardia and drowsiness in a person with type 1 diabetes indicate diabetic ketoacidosis, which is a life-threatening emergency. The only appropriate disposition is emergency transport. Sugar by mouth treats hypoglycemia, not ketoacidosis, and outpatient testing or rest at home loses time the patient does not have.
  38. The correct answer is B (In the constitutional section of the review of systems, not the past medical history): The review of systems lists current symptoms by organ system, and unexplained weight loss, fever, fatigue and night sweats belong to its constitutional section. Past medical history holds diagnosed conditions and the surgical history holds operations such as the appendectomy. Social history records occupation, habits and exposures, not symptoms. These constitutional findings also raise concern for malignancy or infection.
  39. The correct answer is B (Compression, with direct disc pressure deforming the root, unlike stretch of a tethered root): Imaging shows the disc directly contacting the root at the foramen, so the compression model applies. Traction implies stretch of a root that is tethered by scar or adhesion as the spine moves. Both models can cause sensory and motor signs, and they differ in mechanism, not level.
  40. The correct answer is D (GLUT4, the transporter stored in vesicles and inserted into the membrane after insulin signaling): Insulin signaling moves GLUT4 vesicles to the plasma membrane of skeletal muscle and adipocytes, which is why these tissues take up glucose mainly after a meal. GLUT1 works independent of insulin in red cells and the brain barrier. GLUT2 is insulin independent in liver and beta cells, and SGLT1 absorbs luminal glucose by secondary active transport.
  41. The correct answer is D (The Breslow thickness, measured from the granular layer to the deepest tumor cells): Breslow thickness, the vertical depth of invasion in millimeters, is the strongest predictor of outcome and guides margins and sentinel node biopsy. The ABCDE criteria of asymmetry, border, color, diameter and evolution help recognize a suspicious lesion but do not stage it. Age and site have some effect, yet depth outweighs them.
  42. The correct answer is B (Duodenal ulcer in the first duodenal part, linked to excess acid secretion and rare malignancy): Pain relieved by food, nocturnal pain, weight gain and H. pylori point to a duodenal ulcer, usually in the first part, driven by hyperacidity. Gastric ulcers hurt after eating, cause weight loss and carry malignant risk. Curling ulcers follow burns, and Zollinger-Ellison disease produces multiple, atypical ulcers.
  43. The correct answer is D (Type II, in which antibodies against streptococcal M protein cross-react with cardiac antigens and activate complement): Rheumatic fever follows group A streptococcal pharyngitis through molecular mimicry: antibodies to M protein bind cardiac myosin and valve tissue and fix complement, which is type II hypersensitivity. Type I is IgE-mediated and immediate. Type III involves soluble immune complexes, as in poststreptococcal glomerulonephritis. Type IV is delayed T cell reaction, as in tuberculin testing.
  44. The correct answer is D (Arrange emergency transfer to a hospital for urgent MRI and surgical evaluation): Saddle anesthesia, urinary retention, reduced sphincter tone and bilateral leg weakness indicate cauda equina syndrome, a surgical emergency in which decompression within about 24 to 48 hours affects bladder and bowel recovery. The patient needs emergency transfer for MRI and surgical evaluation. Radiographs cannot show the compression, and delays of days or weeks risk permanent loss of function.
  45. The correct answer is A (Unopposed lipolysis floods the liver with fatty acids, and surplus acetyl CoA becomes ketone bodies): Without insulin, hormone-sensitive lipase is unrestrained and adipose tissue releases large amounts of fatty acids. In the liver, beta-oxidation produces more acetyl CoA than the citric acid cycle can use, since oxaloacetate is drawn into gluconeogenesis. The surplus acetyl CoA condenses into acetoacetate and beta-hydroxybutyrate, which are acids and lower the pH. Glucose is not converted directly to ketones, and glucagon inhibits rather than activates pyruvate dehydrogenase.
  46.   47. The correct answer is B (C3 through C6, the typical cervical segments that carry bifid spinous processes): Bifid spinous processes, a triangular foramen, uncinate lips and foramina transversaria together mark the typical cervical vertebrae, C3 through C6. C7 has a long single spinous process and only a small transverse foramen that does not carry the vertebral artery. The atlas has no body and the axis has a dens, so neither matches. Upper thoracic vertebrae have costal facets and a rounder canal.
  48. The correct answer is C (Vertebral osteomyelitis or discitis; urgent referral for emergency evaluation and no spinal manipulation): Constant night pain, fever, weight loss, focal spinal tenderness and recent intravenous drug use are strong risk factors for vertebral osteomyelitis or discitis, which requires urgent medical referral with no spinal manipulation. A strain would not cause fever and weight loss. Ankylosing spondylitis is chronic and does not cause fever or follow injection drug use. Renal colic is colicky and does not cause focal spinal percussion tenderness.
  49. The correct answer is A (Right atrium, forming the convex border between the superior vena cava and the diaphragm): On a PA chest film, the right heart border is formed by the right atrium, continuing upward as the superior vena cava. The right ventricle is anterior and is seen as the retrosternal fill on a lateral view, not as the frontal right border. The left atrium is posterior and only appears as a double density when enlarged. The ascending aorta forms a more superior right contour when it is elongated.
  50. The correct answer is C (Dermatomyositis, a complement-mediated microangiopathy of muscle capillaries with CD4 T cell and B cell infiltrate): Dermatomyositis combines proximal weakness with a heliotrope rash and Gottron papules, and biopsy shows perimysial and perivascular inflammation with perifascicular atrophy from complement-mediated capillary injury. Polymyositis has endomysial CD8 T cell invasion without skin disease. Inclusion body myositis shows rimmed vacuoles in older men with distal and quadriceps weakness, and statin necrotizing myopathy lacks the rash and the perimysial inflammation.
  51. The correct answer is C (Medial meniscus tear with a displaced fragment causing mechanical locking): Joint line tenderness, locking, slowly developing effusion and a painful McMurray click indicate a meniscal tear, most often the medial meniscus. Locking suggests a displaced fragment blocking motion. An anterior cruciate rupture usually produces a rapid hemarthrosis within hours and instability.   52. The correct answer is B (Aponeurosis of the external oblique, folded back on itself along its lower edge): The inguinal ligament is the folded lower edge of the external oblique aponeurosis, stretching between the anterior superior iliac spine and the pubic tubercle. The internal oblique and transversus abdominis contribute the conjoint tendon instead. Transversalis fascia forms the deep ring and the femoral sheath rather than the ligament.
  53. The correct answer is C (Calcific aortic valve stenosis causing fixed left ventricular outflow obstruction): A crescendo-decrescendo ejection murmur at the right upper sternal border radiating to the carotids, with exertional syncope and a slow-rising pulse, marks aortic stenosis. Mitral regurgitation is holosystolic and radiates to the axilla. Hypertrophic cardiomyopathy gives a brisk upstroke and a murmur that increases with Valsalva and decreases with squatting. Pulmonic stenosis is heard at the left upper sternal border and does not radiate to the carotids.
  54. The correct answer is B (Swollen leg needs urgent referral within days, while the chest pain needs emergency department transfer now): Emergency referral applies when delay could cost life, as with crushing chest pain suggesting myocardial infarction, so he goes to the emergency department now. A stable patient with a swollen leg suggesting deep vein thrombosis needs prompt diagnostic testing, which is the urgent tier. Treating both as emergencies blurs the tiers. Reversing them or sending both to outpatient care under-triages the cardiac patient.
  55. The correct answer is A (Iron deficiency anemia from chronic menstrual blood loss with depleted iron stores): Low hemoglobin, low MCV, high RDW, very low ferritin and a high total iron binding capacity show depleted iron stores with compensatory transferrin production. Heavy menses are the likely source of loss. Anemia of chronic disease has a normal or high ferritin and low binding capacity. Thalassemia trait has normal ferritin and a narrow RDW, and sideroblastic anemia has high iron stores.
  56. The correct answer is B (L5 to S1 herniation compressing the traversing S1 root): Lateral foot sensation, plantar flexion and the Achilles reflex belong to S1, the traversing root at the L5 to S1 disc, which a posterolateral herniation compresses. L5 findings would show dorsal foot numbness and great toe extension weakness, and L4 findings would reduce the patellar reflex. The exiting L5 root is spared unless the herniation is far lateral.
  57. The correct answer is D (Awareness of a pulsating sensation in the abdomen when lying flat or sitting still): A pulsating sensation in the abdomen, together with age, smoking, hypertension and constant nonmechanical pain, strongly suggests an abdominal aortic aneurysm. The other items describe position-dependent or activity-dependent patterns of lumbar stenosis, or an inflammatory back pain pattern, none of which fits constant throbbing pain unaffected by posture. The patient needs palpation of the aorta and urgent imaging.
  58. The correct answer is C (Frequent urination, excessive thirst, unintended weight loss and intermittent blurred vision.): Polyuria, polydipsia, weight loss and blurred vision are the classic features of hyperglycemia in new onset diabetes. High glucose spills into urine, drawing water and causing thirst, and swelling of the eye lens blurs vision. The other clusters fit hyperthyroidism, hypothyroidism and renal colic. Each shares one symptom with diabetes but lacks the full pattern.
  59. The correct answer is A (Proximal convoluted tubule, through sodium coupled cotransport and isosmotic water movement driven by basolateral Na-K ATPase): The proximal tubule reabsorbs roughly 65 percent of filtered sodium and water along with nearly all glucose and amino acids, using sodium coupled cotransport. Water follows isosmotically, so tubular fluid stays isosmotic with plasma. Later segments reabsorb smaller fractions and do not reclaim glucose or amino acids.
  60. The correct answer is A (Os odontoideum, a long-standing ununited ossicle that may signal atlantoaxial instability): Os odontoideum has smooth corticated margins, a wide gap, and a rounded ossicle, and no swelling, which indicates a chronic process rather than injury. It can allow atlantoaxial instability, so flexion and extension views or advanced imaging and avoidance of cervical manipulation follow. An acute dens fracture has sharp edges and prevertebral swelling. A Jefferson fracture involves C1, and the ossiculum terminale is a tiny apical ossicle.
  61. The correct answer is B (A 74-year-old woman with a DEXA T-score of minus 3.4 and a prior vertebral compression fracture): The crossed-arm supine push loads the anterior chest wall and thoracic vertebral bodies through the doctor's body weight, so a fragile skeleton is at risk for rib or vertebral fracture. Severe osteoporosis with an existing compression fracture is therefore a major contraindication to this setup. Mechanical stiffness, a mild curve and controlled blood pressure do not by themselves forbid the technique.
  62. The correct answer is A (Delirium, an acute reversible disturbance of attention and cognition triggered by infection or drugs): Acute onset over days, fluctuating alertness, inattention and visual hallucinations in a hospitalized patient with infection define delirium. It is reversible once the cause is treated. Alzheimer disease develops over years with alertness preserved. Vascular dementia declines stepwise after strokes, and pseudodementia leaves attention intact.
  63. The correct answer is D (Subarachnoid hemorrhage from a ruptured aneurysm, with urgent noncontrast head CT): A thunderclap headache reaching peak intensity within seconds, with vomiting and meningismus, indicates subarachnoid hemorrhage until proven otherwise, usually from a ruptured saccular aneurysm. Noncontrast CT within 6 hours of onset is nearly 100 percent sensitive. Migraine and tension headache build gradually. Cluster headache is brief and recurrent with autonomic features, and triptans can be harmful here.
  64. The correct answer is C (Tuberculous spondylitis, preserving disc height early because the organism lacks proteolytic enzymes): Tuberculous spondylitis commonly collapses anterior vertebral bodies and produces a gibbus, and the disc is relatively preserved early because Mycobacterium tuberculosis lacks the proteolytic enzymes of pyogenic bacteria. A paravertebral soft tissue mass or abscess is common. Pyogenic infection erodes the endplate and narrows the disc early. Metastasis and myeloma do not fit the systemic infectious picture.
  65. The correct answer is D (First fits a right Bell palsy; second fits a left cortical or internal capsule lesion): A peripheral lesion of the facial nerve, as in Bell palsy, paralyzes the whole ipsilateral face including the forehead. A central lesion spares the forehead because the upper face receives bilateral cortical input, and the lower face droops on the side opposite the lesion. A right lower face droop with forehead sparing therefore points to a left central lesion. The two patients cannot share one lesion type.
  66. The correct answer is C (Spinal accessory nerve, which supplies the trapezius muscle from the roof of the posterior triangle): The spinal accessory nerve crosses the posterior triangle superficially, where it is vulnerable to node biopsy, and it supplies sternocleidomastoid and trapezius. Trapezius paralysis causes shoulder drooping, an inability to shrug and weak abduction above horizontal because it rotates the scapula. The long thoracic, dorsal scapular and suprascapular nerves supply different muscles and run deeper, away from the posterior triangle roof.
  67. The correct answer is B (Vitamin B12 deficiency, which also impairs methylmalonyl CoA mutase and damages the dorsal columns): Macrocytosis with hypersegmented neutrophils indicates megaloblastic anemia, and the neurological signs with vibration loss separate B12 from folate deficiency. Without B12, methylmalonic acid accumulates and dorsal column and corticospinal damage follows. Folate deficiency spares the nervous system. Iron deficiency is microcytic, and copper deficiency is a rare mimic in someone without malabsorption or zinc excess.
  68. The correct answer is B (Reduced delivery of trophic proteins along the axon, altering the health of the tissue supplied): Normal conduction studies exclude demyelinating compression as the explanation. The proposal describes slowed axoplasmic flow that deprives the target tissue of trophic substances, which is the axonal aberration or trophic model. Segmental facilitation is a separate model about excitability, and vasa nervorum ischemia would be expected to cause pain and conduction change.
  69. The correct answer is B (Wash the site with soap and water, then report immediately for medical evaluation and baseline testing): First aid is to wash the wound with soap and water and flush mucous membranes with water, without squeezing or using caustic agents. Prompt reporting matters because postexposure prophylaxis for HIV works best within hours, and evaluation includes baseline testing, source patient testing, and her hepatitis B antibody status. Waiting for symptoms loses the window, and revaccinating without checking antibody and source results skips the decision that determines whether immune globulin is needed.
  70. The correct answer is A (The linear order of amino acids joined by peptide bonds in the polypeptide chain): Heat supplies energy that disrupts noncovalent forces such as hydrogen bonds, hydrophobic contacts and ionic interactions. That unfolds secondary, tertiary and any quaternary structure, which is denaturation. Peptide bonds are covalent and need strong acid or enzymes to break, so the primary sequence stays intact. Because the sequence is preserved, some proteins can refold when conditions are gentle, although cooked egg white does not.
  71. The correct answer is A (Foraminal narrowing with irritation of a right cervical nerve root, a positive Spurling test): Extension, ipsilateral side bending and axial compression narrow the intervertebral foramen on the tested side. Reproduction of radiating arm pain therefore points to nerve root compression, commonly from disc material or uncovertebral spurring. The Adson test assesses the radial pulse during neck rotation and deep inspiration. The Sharp-Purser test checks the atlantodental relationship, and the Tinel sign is a percussion test at the wrist.
  72. The correct answer is A (Inspect, auscultate, percuss, palpate, because pressing on the abdomen first can change bowel sounds): The abdomen is the one region where palpation and percussion come after auscultation, because pressing or tapping can stimulate or quiet peristalsis and make bowel sounds misleading. Inspection still comes first because it costs nothing and may show distension, scars or visible peristalsis. Starting with palpation or percussion is tempting because it feels more informative, but it risks altering the sounds you listen to afterward. Starting with auscultation before looking skips the free information inspection provides.
  73. The correct answer is A (Thumb on the posterior radial head, low-amplitude force directed posterior to anterior): To restore a posterior to anterior radial head glide, the contact is on the posterior radial head and the low-amplitude force is applied in that same restricted direction. A reversed anterior contact would push the head further into the restriction. The olecranon belongs to the ulna, and a contact on the lateral epicondyle irritates the inflamed common extensor tendon without moving the radiocapitellar joint.
  74. The correct answer is D (Auscultate bowel sounds before percussion and palpation, because handling the abdomen changes bowel sound frequency): Inspection, auscultation, percussion and palpation is the standard abdominal order because percussion and palpation stimulate or suppress peristalsis and can change the bowel sounds the examiner then hears. Listening first gives an unaltered baseline. Palpating first to map tenderness or percussing first to outline gas would both disturb that baseline, and the claim that order has no effect on bowel sounds is false.
  75. The correct answer is A (The cord ends near L1 to L2, so L3 to L4 contains only nerve roots): In adults the spinal cord tapers into the conus medullaris near L1 to L2, so a needle at L3 to L4 meets only the cauda equina, whose roots float in cerebrospinal fluid and tend to be pushed aside. The dural sac actually extends to about S2, so the subarachnoid space is still reached at L3 to L4. A wide canal and upward angled spinous processes do affect technique, but neither is what protects the cord.
  76. The correct answer is A (The breasts sit farther from the tube, reducing dose to radiosensitive breast tissue): In the PA projection the beam enters through the back and exits through the front, so the anterior breast tissue is farthest from the source. This lowers breast dose in a growing girl who needs repeated films, and studies show the reduction is substantial. Thyroid and gonadal protection comes from shielding and collimation, not from changing projection, and the tissue thickness the beam crosses is the same in both directions.
  77. The correct answer is C (Myasthenia gravis, an autoimmune disorder of neuromuscular junction acetylcholine receptors): Fatigable weakness that worsens late in the day and improves with rest or cooling, with normal pupils, is characteristic of myasthenia gravis. Antibodies block acetylcholine receptors at the neuromuscular junction. Horner syndrome causes miosis and a fixed mild ptosis. A compressive oculomotor palsy dilates the pupil, and Lambert-Eaton weakness improves with repeated effort.
  78. The correct answer is A (Use a long exposure time while the patient breathes quietly and shallowly during the exposure): A long exposure with shallow, quiet breathing lets lung vessels move during the exposure, so the markings blur while the stationary spine stays sharp. Holding the breath with a short exposure freezes the markings and leaves them superimposed on the vertebrae. Increasing kVp lowers contrast but does not blur lung structures, and anode speed has no bearing on this effect.
  79. The correct answer is D (BTK gene mutation that blocks pre-B cell maturation, with very low levels of all immunoglobulin classes): X-linked agammaglobulinemia results from mutation of Bruton tyrosine kinase, so B cells stop at the pre-B stage. Infection begins after about six months when maternal IgG wanes, and all immunoglobulins are low. In hyper-IgM, B cells are present. Selective IgA deficiency preserves IgG, and adenosine deaminase deficiency affects T cells as well.
  80. The correct answer is C (Aggravating and relieving factors, such as positions or movements that change the pain): The narrative already gives onset (three weeks, lifting plywood), location (right lumbar), quality (aching), severity (6 of 10), radiation (right buttock) and prior episodes. What is missing is what makes the pain better or worse, along with its timing through the day. Those facts guide mechanical versus inflammatory reasoning and are required to complete the present illness.
  81. The correct answer is D (Strengthen deep neck flexors and lower trapezius while stretching pectoralis minor and upper trapezius): Upper crossed syndrome pairs tight pectorals, upper trapezius and levator scapulae and suboccipitals with weak deep neck flexors and lower trapezius. Correction lengthens the tight set and strengthens the weak set, which restores scapular position and a neutral head. The wrong plans each train the overactive group or stretch the weak group, which deepens the forward head posture.
  82. The correct answer is D (Pleural effusion, with fluid layering at the base and compressed lung above producing egophony): Dullness, reduced fremitus and absent breath sounds over the base point to fluid in the pleural space that separates lung from chest wall. Egophony at the upper border arises where the compressed lung abuts the fluid. Consolidation transmits sound and increases fremitus, pneumothorax is hyperresonant, and lobar collapse shifts the trachea toward the lesion and does not produce a fluid line with egophony above it.
  83. The correct answer is B (Gout from monosodium urate crystals, which are needle-shaped and strongly negatively birefringent under polarized light): Needle-shaped crystals that are strongly negatively birefringent are monosodium urate, diagnostic of gout, and podagra after alcohol is the classic presentation. Calcium pyrophosphate crystals are rhomboid and weakly positively birefringent, usually affecting the knee of an older patient. A synovial count near 28,000 is typical of crystal arthritis, and the negative Gram stain with normal glucose makes infection unlikely, although crystals do not fully exclude it.
  84. The correct answer is D (Thick ascending limb of the loop of Henle, blocking the Na-K-2Cl cotransporter in the luminal membrane): Furosemide blocks the Na-K-2Cl cotransporter on the luminal side of the thick ascending limb, which reabsorbs about a quarter of filtered sodium and drives the medullary gradient. Blocking it reduces the positive lumen potential that drives calcium and magnesium reabsorption, producing calciuria. Thiazides act at the distal Na-Cl cotransporter, amiloride at collecting duct sodium channels, and acetazolamide in the proximal tubule.
  85. The correct answer is A (Five of nine symptoms, including depressed mood or anhedonia, present nearly daily for two weeks): A major depressive episode needs five or more of nine symptoms during the same two week period, and at least one must be depressed mood or loss of interest. The symptoms must also cause distress or impairment. Fewer symptoms or a shorter course does not meet the threshold, and six months is the generalized anxiety duration.
  86. The correct answer is A (2 mM, the substrate concentration at which velocity equals half of the maximal velocity): Km is defined as the substrate concentration at which velocity is half of Vmax. Half of 80 micromoles per minute is 40, and the velocity of 40 was observed at 2 mM substrate, so Km equals 2 mM. Vmax is a velocity measured in micromoles per minute and not a concentration. The other values do not satisfy the definition.
  87. The correct answer is D (Basal cell carcinoma, a locally invasive tumor that rarely metastasizes): A pearly papule with rolled borders and telangiectasia on sun-exposed skin is classic basal cell carcinoma, which grows locally and rarely spreads. Squamous cell carcinoma is typically scaly or ulcerated and can metastasize to nodes. Melanoma is pigmented, and actinic keratosis is a rough scaly patch without a rolled pearly edge.
  88. The correct answer is D (Rapid separation of joint surfaces creates a gas cavity in synovial fluid, producing the pop): Cavitation occurs when a distraction of the synovial joint surfaces rapidly lowers pressure and a gas cavity forms in the fluid, producing the audible pop. The sound does not prove that adhesions were released or that a lesion was corrected. Ligament snapping and bubble collapse are alternative explanations that are not the accepted account.
  89. The correct answer is C (Stop the examination, position her on her left side and activate emergency transport): Sudden severe abdominal pain with a rigid abdomen and vaginal bleeding in the third trimester suggests placental abruption, which endangers both mother and fetus within minutes. The patient is positioned on her left side to improve venous return and emergency services are activated at once. Any treatment, heat or further examination delays definitive care and is inappropriate.
  90. The correct answer is A (Begin conservative care without plain films, since no red flag features are present): Acute low back pain in a young adult with no red flags is usually self limited, and early imaging does not improve outcomes. Guidelines support imaging only when red flags such as trauma, malignancy history, infection signs, progressive deficit or cauda equina features appear, or when pain fails to respond after about four to six weeks. Plain films, MRI and stress views all add cost and, for films, radiation without altering the plan of care here.
  91. The correct answer is C (Pancoast tumor of the lung apex involving the sympathetic chain and lower brachial plexus roots): Ptosis, miosis, and anhidrosis form Horner syndrome, and combined with shoulder and arm pain and hand intrinsic weakness in a heavy smoker they indicate a Pancoast tumor. The apical mass invades the sympathetic chain at the stellate ganglion and the C8 to T1 roots. A C6 herniation does not cause Horner signs. Thoracic outlet syndrome lacks sympathetic findings, and cluster headache does not weaken the hand.
  92. The correct answer is B (Gout, with punched-out erosions and overhanging edges at the first metatarsophalangeal joint): Gout classically produces punched-out erosions with sclerotic, overhanging edges, often with preserved joint space until late and soft tissue swelling from tophi. Rheumatoid erosions are marginal with periarticular osteopenia, and hallux osteoarthritis has narrowing and osteophytes but no erosions. Septic arthritis destroys cortex and joint space rapidly. Thiazide diuretics raise uric acid, which supports gout.
  93. The correct answer is A (Lyme disease from Borrelia burgdorferi, now in early disseminated stage with seventh cranial nerve involvement): Erythema migrans after a tick bite in an endemic area establishes early Lyme disease, and later facial nerve palsy is a typical early disseminated manifestation. Rocky Mountain spotted fever causes a petechial rash that starts distally and spreads centrally. Ehrlichiosis presents with fever and cytopenias without migrating annular rash. Bell palsy is idiopathic, and the antecedent rash here is the stem detail that points to Borrelia.
  94. The correct answer is D (Potassium, since open leak channels make the resting membrane far more permeable to K+ than Na+): At rest the membrane is far more permeable to potassium than to sodium because of K+ leak channels, so the potential sits near the K+ equilibrium of about -90 mV. A small sodium leak and the electrogenic pump bring it to about -70 mV. The Na+/K+ ATPase mainly maintains the gradients over time. Voltage-gated calcium channels are closed at rest.
  95. The correct answer is D (Graves disease with hyperthyroidism, referred to a physician for thyroid labs and medical treatment): Weight loss with a good appetite, heat intolerance, tremor, tachycardia, a diffuse goiter and exophthalmos form the classic picture of Graves disease, an autoimmune hyperthyroidism needing medical testing and treatment. Hashimoto thyroiditis typically presents with hypothyroid features such as cold intolerance and weight gain. Anxiety does not explain a goiter or exophthalmos, and pheochromocytoma would not be treated conservatively.
  96. The correct answer is D (Systemic lupus erythematosus, a type III hypersensitivity with immune complexes in skin, joints and glomeruli): A malar rash, photosensitivity, arthralgia, proteinuria and anti-dsDNA antibodies define systemic lupus erythematosus. Immune complex deposition produces the skin, joint and kidney disease. Dermatomyositis features muscle weakness and anti-Jo-1, rheumatoid arthritis features rheumatoid factor and anti-CCP, and systemic sclerosis features skin tightening.
  97. The correct answer is C (Provocative and palliative factors, meaning the activities or positions that worsen or relieve symptoms): Provocative factors are the movements or positions that bring on or worsen pain, and palliative factors are those that relieve it. Asking what makes the complaint better or worse is exactly this element. Quality describes the character of the pain, region and radiation describe its location and spread, and timing and severity describe duration and intensity. These elements are all part of a complete pain analysis, though each is a separate question.
  98. The correct answer is A (Acute gout, with negatively birefringent needle-shaped crystals in joint fluid and elevated serum uric acid): Sudden podagra after alcohol and purine-rich food in a middle-aged man is classic acute gout from monosodium urate crystals. Joint aspiration shows needle-shaped crystals with negative birefringence, and serum uric acid is usually elevated. Pseudogout shows positively birefringent rhomboid crystals and usually affects the knee. Septic arthritis is possible but the low-grade temperature and trigger favor gout, and osteoarthritis does not flare this acutely.

Practice Test 4 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is C (Possible miscounting of lumbar levels, with added stress and degeneration at the segment above): A lumbosacral transitional vertebra changes the apparent level count, so a surgeon or manipulator can identify the wrong segment unless counting from C2 or the T12 rib is used. The altered mechanics also load the segment above, which may degenerate earlier. The anomaly does not cause a pars defect, and a winking owl sign suggests pedicle destruction rather than this variant.
  2. The correct answer is B (Decreased plasma oncotic pressure, which reduces the force retaining fluid and favors filtration out of capillaries): Albumin generates most plasma oncotic pressure, which holds fluid in the capillary. In nephrotic syndrome urinary albumin loss lowers oncotic pressure, so net filtration exceeds reabsorption and fluid accumulates in the interstitium, with swelling first appearing in low pressure areas like the face. Raised oncotic pressure or raised interstitial pressure would reduce edema, and lower permeability would not cause it.
  3. The correct answer is C (Osteochondroma, with continuity of cortex and marrow; pain and growth after maturity suggest chondrosarcoma): An osteochondroma is a pedunculated or sessile exostosis whose cortex and medullary cavity are continuous with the parent bone, and it points away from the nearest joint. It is a benign tumor in an adolescent. New pain, growth after skeletal maturity or a thickened cartilage cap suggests degeneration to secondary chondrosarcoma. Surface osteosarcoma and myositis ossificans do not have marrow continuity, and enchondroma is central.
  4. The correct answer is A (Tay-Sachs disease from accumulated GM2 ganglioside within neuronal lysosomes due to absent hexosaminidase A enzyme activity): Tay-Sachs disease is hexosaminidase A deficiency, so GM2 ganglioside accumulates in neurons and causes regression, hyperacusis, and a cherry red macula. The absence of hepatosplenomegaly separates it from Niemann-Pick disease, which can share the macular spot. Gaucher disease causes organomegaly and bone pain rather than early neurodegeneration with a macular spot. Krabbe disease shows globoid cells and neuropathy, not a cherry red macula.
  5. The correct answer is B (Correct it openly, leaving the original readable and adding initials and the date): A correction must keep the original entry legible and show who changed it, what was changed and when, so the record stays trustworthy. Deleting leaves no trace of the change, leaving a known error invites later mistakes in care, and writing that left was correct would add a second inaccuracy to the chart.
  6. The correct answer is D (Osteoarthritis of the hands, with Heberden nodes and sparing of the metacarpophalangeal joints): Heberden nodes are bony enlargements of the distal interphalangeal joints and mark hand osteoarthritis. Brief morning stiffness, normal MCP joints and no warmth separate it from inflammatory disease. Rheumatoid arthritis spares the DIP joints and gives long stiffness. Psoriatic arthritis would bring nail pitting or plaques, and gout would produce an acutely inflamed joint.
  7. The correct answer is D (Thick skin of the palms and soles, below the corneum): The stratum lucidum is a thin clear layer of flattened, keratinized cells found only in thick skin, the palms and soles. Thin skin has only four layers and lacks it. Mucous membranes and scalp skin do not have a stratum lucidum either.
  8. The correct answer is C (Vitamin B12 deficiency; supplement B12 because it occurs naturally only in animal foods): A macrocytic picture with hypersegmented neutrophils and sensory symptoms in a strict vegan points to B12 deficiency, because B12 comes naturally only from animal foods. The normal folate level argues against folate deficiency, and iron deficiency produces small cells, not an MCV of 108. Supplementing B12 is the correct intervention.
  9. The correct answer is A (L5 root compressed by an L4 to L5 posterolateral herniation): Great toe extension weakness and dorsal foot sensory loss point to the L5 root, which is the traversing root at the L4 to L5 disc and is hit by a posterolateral herniation there. S1 involvement would reduce the Achilles reflex and lateral foot sensation. The L4 root affects the patellar reflex and medial leg.
  10. The correct answer is C (Demyelination of the dorsal columns and peripheral nerves from impaired methylmalonyl-CoA metabolism): Vitamin B12 is needed to convert methylmalonyl-CoA to succinyl-CoA, and its lack allows abnormal fatty acids into myelin, causing subacute combined degeneration of the dorsal columns and peripheral nerves. Vegans are at risk because B12 comes from animal foods. Folate deficiency causes the same macrocytic anemia but spares the nervous system. Iron deficiency is microcytic, and thiamine deficiency produces a different neurologic and cardiac picture.
  11. The correct answer is D (The inferior pubic ramus border and the inferior femoral neck border): The Shenton line is drawn along the inferior border of the superior pubic ramus and continues across the obturator foramen to the inferior border of the femoral neck. In a normal hip this forms one smooth arc, and a break or step suggests dislocation, femoral neck fracture, or slipped epiphysis. The other pairs name real pelvic landmarks but are not the structures this arc connects.
  12. The correct answer is D (Probable breast carcinoma, so arrange diagnostic mammography and referral for tissue biopsy without delay): A hard, fixed, irregular, nontender mass with skin dimpling and an axillary node is carcinoma until proved otherwise. Diagnostic imaging and biopsy are needed promptly. Fibroadenomas are mobile and rubbery, fibrocystic changes are tender and cyclic, and papillomas present with discharge. None of these explains fixation and skin dimpling.
  13. The correct answer is A (Posterior facet joint irritation on the right, with no evidence of nerve root compression): Extension with rotation toward the painful side closes the posterior facet joint and narrows the foramen. Pain that stays local in the low back points to the facet joint. Radiating leg pain would be needed to suggest foraminal compression of a nerve root. A central herniation would produce bilateral or saddle findings, which are absent here.
  14. The correct answer is A (Skeletal muscle lacks glucose 6-phosphatase, so glucose 6-phosphate stays trapped inside the muscle cell): Muscle glycogen is degraded by phosphorylase to glucose 1-phosphate, then to glucose 6-phosphate, which enters glycolysis for local use. Muscle lacks glucose 6-phosphatase, so the phosphorylated sugar cannot leave the cell as free glucose. Only the liver, with that enzyme, can release glucose to blood. Muscle does have phosphorylase and hexokinase, and it responds to epinephrine rather than glucagon.
  15. The correct answer is B (Cervical lordosis at 3-4 months with head lifting, lumbar lordosis at 9-12 months): The thoracic and sacral kyphoses are primary curves present at birth. The cervical lordosis forms when the infant lifts the head against gravity at about 3 to 4 months. The lumbar lordosis follows as the infant sits and then stands and walks, from about 9 to 12 months. The other pairings reverse the sequence or place the milestones years too late.
  16. The correct answer is A (Document both readings, explain the elevated pressure, and refer for medical evaluation before treating): Two correct readings on a rested seated patient confirm sustained stage 2 level hypertension, so the doctor documents the values, informs the patient, and refers for medical evaluation before treatment. The readings are below 180/120 and he has no symptoms of end organ injury, so emergency transport is not needed. Walking before the repeat would add exercise effect and invalidate the measurement, and proceeding with treatment first would put a pressure that needs medical assessment ahead of the plan.
  17. The correct answer is B (Left ischial tuberosity contact with a thrust directed anterior and superior): A posterior-inferior ilium is moved back toward its neutral position by driving it anterior and superior, and the ischial tuberosity gives a firm contact that carries that vector. A PSIS contact with a posterior-inferior thrust is the correction for the opposite listing and would worsen this one. Contacting the ASIS or the iliac crest with the stated lines of drive does not move the ilium along the line of misalignment.
  18. The correct answer is C (Abdominal aortic aneurysm with calcification of the vessel wall): A thin curvilinear calcified rim just anterior to the spine, over 3 cm, in an older smoker with constant nonmechanical pain, is an abdominal aortic aneurysm until proven otherwise. Calcified nodes appear as clumped, mottled densities, and stones are dense, solid and usually rounded. Osteophytes arise from vertebral margins and are bone density, not a free standing arc. The next step is prompt vascular ultrasound or CT angiography.
  19. The correct answer is B (Nutritional rickets from vitamin D deficiency with impaired mineralization of growth plate cartilage and osteoid): Reduced vitamin D lowers calcium and phosphate absorption, so osteoid and growth plate cartilage fail to mineralize, producing bowing, widened physes, and cupped, frayed metaphyses. Compensatory hyperparathyroidism and raised alkaline phosphatase follow. X-linked hypophosphatemic rickets looks similar but vitamin D is normal. Osteogenesis imperfecta and achondroplasia do not produce rachitic metaphyseal changes.
  20. The correct answer is D (A subpubic angle over 80 to 90 degrees with a broad, shallow pelvic cavity): The female pelvis is adapted for childbirth, so the subpubic angle exceeds 80 to 90 degrees and the cavity is broad and shallow with a rounded or oval inlet. The male pelvis has a subpubic angle near 60 degrees, a deep funnel-shaped cavity, a heart-shaped inlet and heavier bones. Each of the wrong choices is a standard male trait.
  21. The correct answer is D (Muscle protein catabolism supplies amino acids for hepatic gluconeogenesis, while peripheral insulin sensitivity is reduced): Glucocorticoids break down muscle protein and send the amino acids to the liver for gluconeogenesis, which also explains proximal wasting and thin skin. They antagonize insulin in peripheral tissue, so glucose rises. Anabolic muscle effects and enhanced insulin release are the opposite of cortisol action, and aldosterone does not account for the myopathy or the diabetes-like picture.
  22. The correct answer is C (Primary spontaneous pneumothorax, shown by a visceral pleural line with absent peripheral markings): A tall, thin young man with sudden pleuritic pain and dyspnea fits a primary spontaneous pneumothorax from rupture of an apical bleb. The film shows a thin visceral pleural line with no lung markings peripheral to it. Tension pneumothorax would cause shift, hypotension and tracheal deviation, none of which are present. Effusion and pneumonia produce opacities, not a lucent space without markings.
  23. The correct answer is B (Measles, mumps, and rubella vaccine, a live attenuated preparation postponed until after delivery): Live attenuated vaccines such as measles, mumps, and rubella contain replicating virus and are contraindicated in pregnancy because of a theoretical fetal risk, so she should be vaccinated after delivery. Inactivated influenza, Tdap, and recombinant hepatitis B vaccines contain killed or purified components that cannot replicate, so they are recommended or acceptable when indicated. Her inactivated influenza shot was therefore appropriate and needs no correction.
  24. The correct answer is C (Superior thyroid artery, which descends to supply the upper pole of the thyroid gland): The superior thyroid artery is the first branch of the external carotid artery, arising near the carotid bifurcation and descending to the upper pole of the thyroid. The lingual artery arises next at the level of the hyoid. The facial artery arises above it and crosses the mandible. The occipital artery arises still higher and passes to the posterior scalp.
  25. The correct answer is A (Half a segment upward, since the tip lies half a segment below its own transverse process): In the rule of threes, the spinous tips of T1 to T3 lie at the same level as their own transverse processes, T4 to T6 lie half a segment below, and T7 to T9 lie one full segment below. Therefore at T5 the transverse process lies half a segment above the spinous tip. The examiner moves up, not down, and not the full segment used for lower thoracic levels.
  26. The correct answer is C (Structural scoliosis with vertebral rotation, since the rib prominence persists in forward flexion): A rib hump that persists on forward bending shows fixed vertebral rotation, which defines a structural curve and calls for a standing radiograph and Cobb measurement. Postural scoliosis disappears on flexion because there is no rotation, so a short leg would not explain a persistent hump. Scheuermann kyphosis is a sagittal plane wedging problem and would be seen as a rounded thoracic profile from the side. Reassurance alone would miss a curve that can progress during the adolescent growth spurt.
  27. The correct answer is B (Absence of cold-triggered blanching of the fingertips, with symptoms confined to the median nerve distribution): Both conditions occur in vibrating tool users, so the discriminating feature is the pattern. Carpal tunnel syndrome follows the median distribution, is worse at night and relieved by shaking, with no cold-induced blanching. Cold-triggered white fingers followed by redness is vibration white finger, and work-timing and neck pain features do not separate the two named conditions.
  28. The correct answer is C (Call emergency services immediately, keep him resting, and do not treat his neck): Crushing chest pressure spreading to the arm and jaw with diaphoresis and nausea suggests acute coronary syndrome and calls for emergency services at once, with the patient kept at rest. Any neck treatment wastes time, and a visit later in the week or the next morning is far too late.
  29. The correct answer is B (Refer urgently for abdominal vascular imaging and withhold manipulation until he is evaluated): A pulsatile abdominal mass with back pain suggests an abdominal aortic aneurysm. He is stable, so urgent vascular imaging is appropriate, and spinal force is withheld until it is excluded. Even light manipulation or traction carries risk with an aneurysm. Lumbar films do not assess the aorta, and waiting for visible enlargement allows rupture risk to continue.
  30. The correct answer is B (One abdominal ultrasound scan measuring aortic diameter, with 3.0 cm or more defining an aneurysm): Men aged 65 to 75 who have ever smoked are offered a single screening abdominal ultrasound, which is inexpensive, noninvasive and highly sensitive. An aortic diameter of 3.0 cm or more is the definition of an aneurysm. CT angiography exposes the patient to radiation and contrast and is reserved for planning repair, while palpation misses small aneurysms. Plain film calcification is neither sensitive nor specific enough to screen.
  31. The correct answer is A (Hold manual in-line stabilization of the head, tell her not to move, and activate EMS): Midline neck pain with bilateral hand tingling after a collision suggests an unstable cervical fracture with possible cord involvement. The priority is to prevent movement by holding the head in neutral in-line position and activating EMS for trained extrication with a rigid collar and board. Moving her, manipulating or driving her risks permanent cord injury.
  32. The correct answer is B (Autoantibodies activate the TSH receptor on follicular cells, driving iodide trapping and hormone synthesis without TSH): Graves disease is caused by stimulating antibodies to the TSH receptor, which increase iodide trapping, thyroid peroxidase activity and hormone release without regard to TSH. The resulting high thyroxine suppresses pituitary TSH. A TSH-secreting adenoma would show high TSH, destructive thyroiditis lacks exophthalmos, and impaired iodide trapping lowers hormone output.
  33. The correct answer is C (Failure of the posterior neural arches to unite in the midline, often at L5 or S1): Spina bifida occulta results from incomplete fusion of the posterior neural arches, and skin markers such as a hair tuft overlie the defect. It is most common at L5 or S1 and can accompany tethering of the cord, which would explain the weakness and bladder symptoms. Cranial neuropore failure produces anencephaly. A butterfly vertebra comes from failed fusion of the body centers.
  34. Compression there gives burning and tingling of the sole. The deep fibular nerve is compressed in the anterior tarsal tunnel, and the sural and superficial fibular nerves supply only the lateral foot and dorsum.
  35. The correct answer is B (Lateral lumbar spine of an average adult, measuring about 30 centimeters through the part): Thick parts generate large amounts of Compton scatter that fogs the film, and a grid of thin lead strips absorbs it and improves contrast at the cost of a higher dose. The usual threshold is about 10 to 12 centimeters of part thickness, and a lateral lumbar spine at about 30 centimeters far exceeds it. The hand, elbow, and slender neck fall below the threshold and are imaged without a grid.
  36. The correct answer is C (Mammillary process of L4 on the upper side, thrust posterior to anterior, slightly inferior to superior): The lumbar facets face roughly sagittally, so a contact on the mammillary process with a posterior to anterior and slightly inferior to superior drive follows the facet plane. The patient lies with the side to be adjusted facing up and the trunk rotated by the arm pull to take up slack. A lateral spinous thrust, a segment below, or a drive along the long axis misses the joint plane and loads the disc instead.
  37. The correct answer is C (An anteroposterior projection with the mouth open and the central ray through the mouth): The open mouth projection moves the teeth and mandible out of the way so the dens and the lateral masses of C1 are seen directly, along with the atlantoaxial joints. The lateral view overlaps these structures. The cephalad-angled lower cervical view targets C3 to C7, and the oblique view is used to evaluate the intervertebral foramina.
  38. The correct answer is C (Muscle inserts GLUT4 and stores glycogen, liver builds glycogen, and adipose lipolysis is suppressed): Insulin signaling moves GLUT4 vesicles to the membrane of muscle and adipose cells, which increases glucose entry. It activates glycogen synthase through dephosphorylation in liver and muscle, so glucose is stored as glycogen. In fat cells it inhibits hormone-sensitive lipase, which lowers fatty acid release. The other combinations describe fasting physiology driven by glucagon and catecholamines, or confuse GLUT2 with the insulin-responsive GLUT4.
  39. The correct answer is B (Avoid contact and heavy lifting for 4 weeks from onset because trauma can rupture the spleen): Mononucleosis enlarges the spleen, and the organ is fragile for several weeks, so blunt trauma or Valsalva strain can cause rupture even after symptoms ease. Contact sports and heavy lifting are generally held for about 4 weeks from onset, with clinical judgment about spleen size. Fever resolution does not show the spleen has returned to normal size. Liver enzyme levels and node size do not measure splenic rupture risk.
  40. The correct answer is D (Deep veins of the left leg, typically the popliteal or femoral veins): Prolonged immobility on a long flight and unilateral calf swelling point to deep vein thrombosis, with most pulmonary emboli arising from the popliteal, femoral, or iliac veins. The embolus passes through the right heart into the pulmonary arteries, causing dyspnea, pleuritic pain, tachycardia, and hypoxemia. Left heart thrombi travel to the systemic circulation, so they cause stroke or limb ischemia rather than a pulmonary event. Superficial veins rarely embolize.
  41. The correct answer is C (Radon, assessed with a test kit placed in the lowest lived-in level): Radon is a radioactive gas from uranium decay in soil and rock that enters through foundations, and it is a leading cause of lung cancer in never smokers. Households test with short-term or long-term kits in the lowest regularly used level and mitigate when results are high. Carbon monoxide causes acute poisoning, asbestos is a fiber, and formaldehyde is a chemical irritant, none of them radioactive.
  42. The correct answer is C (Urinary infection remains likely, because some uropathogens do not reduce nitrate and leave the nitrite negative): Leukocyte esterase of 3+ with 30 to 50 white cells per high-power field, bacteria and typical symptoms indicate cystitis. Nitrite is specific but insensitive, since Enterococcus, Staphylococcus saprophyticus and others do not convert nitrate, and short bladder dwell time also gives false negatives. Glomerulonephritis would show dysmorphic red cells and casts, and lower counts than 100,000 colonies are significant in symptomatic women.
  43. The correct answer is B (The somatovisceral reflex model, where a somatic lesion drives segmental autonomic change in an organ): The stem fixes the direction as lesion first and organ second, which is somatovisceral. Viscerosomatic is the reverse direction, from organ to somatic segment. Somatosomatic reflexes link somatic structures only, and the immune model targets lymphoid tissue, which the stem rules out.
  44. The correct answer is A (Mycobacterium tuberculosis, an acid-fast bacillus that spreads hematogenously to vertebral bodies and initially spares the disc): Subacute back pain, constitutional symptoms, origin from an endemic region, multilevel vertebral destruction and a paraspinal abscess with early disc preservation describe Pott disease from Mycobacterium tuberculosis. Pyogenic Staphylococcus aureus usually presents acutely and destroys the disc early. Brucella involves lumbar vertebrae and needs exposure history. Salmonella osteomyelitis is tied to sickle cell disease.
  45. The correct answer is D (Costodiaphragmatic recess at the lateral lung base, below the lower lobe margin): On an upright film, fluid gravitates to the lowest part of the pleural cavity, the costodiaphragmatic recess, which lung enters only on deep inspiration. About 200 to 300 mL of fluid blunts the lateral costophrenic angle first. The costomediastinal recess is anterior, the fissure fills only when fluid is loculated, and the apex lies above the dependent zone.
  46. The correct answer is B (Ligamentum flavum, the elastin-rich ligament spanning adjacent laminae): The ligamentum flavum joins the laminae of adjacent vertebrae and is the most elastic spinal ligament because of its high elastin content, which gives it a yellow color and helps restore posture after flexion. Thickening of this ligament contributes to lumbar stenosis. The other ligaments are mainly collagenous and less elastic.
  47. The correct answer is D (Diabetes is confirmed because fasting glucose reached 126 mg/dL or higher on two tests.): A fasting plasma glucose of 126 mg/dL or higher, repeated on a second occasion, meets the diagnostic threshold for diabetes mellitus. Prediabetes covers fasting values from 100 to 125 mg/dL, so 132 and 136 lie above it. A cutoff of 140 mg/dL does not apply to fasting samples. A random glucose is not required when two fasting values already qualify. The patient should be referred for medical management.
  48. The correct answer is D (Acetylcholine acts on M2 receptors, opening potassium channels and slowing sinoatrial firing and AV nodal conduction): Vagal stimulation releases acetylcholine onto M2 receptors, which open potassium channels, hyperpolarize the cells and slow phase 4 depolarization. Slowed AV nodal conduction can interrupt a reentrant circuit that depends on the node. Nicotinic receptors are ganglionic or endplate receptors, and beta 1 or alpha 1 actions are sympathetic, not parasympathetic nodal effects.
  49. The correct answer is D (Acute appendicitis, with an inflamed appendix irritating the adjacent parietal peritoneum): Periumbilical pain that migrates to the right lower quadrant, with anorexia, low-grade fever, maximal tenderness at McBurney point and a positive Rovsing sign, is acute appendicitis. Rovsing sign reflects peritoneal irritation. Mesenteric adenitis is diffuse, ureteral colic radiates to the groin with hematuria, and Crohn ileitis has a chronic course.
  50. The correct answer is D (A normal nutrient vessel channel, recognized by its sclerotic margins, oblique course, and symmetric location): A nutrient canal is a vascular channel that passes obliquely through the cortex, and its walls are smooth and sclerotic because it is a normal, long-standing structure. It appears at predictable sites, often on both sides, and has no soft tissue swelling or periosteal response. An acute stress fracture has sharp unmarginated edges and a periosteal reaction, an osteoid osteoma shows a rounded nidus with thick sclerosis, and a cortical infraction would not be symmetric or sclerotic-edged.
  51. The correct answer is A (A father diagnosed with ankylosing spondylitis, since HLA-B27 related disease clusters in families): Insidious back pain in a young man with prolonged morning stiffness that improves with activity suggests axial spondyloarthritis, and a first-degree relative with ankylosing spondylitis raises the probability because HLA-B27 related disease runs in families. Knee osteoarthritis, disc herniation and diabetes in relatives do not support an inflammatory spinal pattern. The pattern here is inflammatory, so family history of a related spondyloarthropathy is the item that changes the pretest probability.
  52. The correct answer is D (Distal symmetric sensorimotor polyneuropathy, a length-dependent axonal process from chronic hyperglycemia): Chronic hyperglycemia damages the longest axons first, producing a symmetric stocking distribution with burning pain and absent ankle reflexes. Spinal stenosis causes leg symptoms with walking that ease with sitting. Tarsal tunnel syndrome is a focal entrapment limited to the sole and heel. Bilateral S1 radiculopathy would follow a root pattern, not an even stocking loss rising to the ankles.
  53. The correct answer is A (Reach about 1,200 mg of calcium daily from food plus supplements, with adequate vitamin D): Postmenopausal women need about 1,200 mg of calcium daily, preferably from food with supplements filling the gap, along with adequate vitamin D for absorption. Intakes near 2,500 mg exceed the safe upper limit and raise stone risk. Staying near 600 mg leaves her short, and avoiding vitamin D undermines calcium absorption.
  54. The correct answer is D (Orthostatic hypotension, because the systolic fell 26 mmHg and exceeded the 20 mmHg criterion): Orthostatic hypotension is a systolic fall of at least 20 mmHg or a diastolic fall of at least 10 mmHg within 3 minutes of standing. Her systolic fell from 128 to 102, a drop of 26 mmHg, so she meets the criterion. Either component is sufficient, so the small diastolic drop of 8 does not rule it out. The standing value of 102 being above 90 does not matter, and the timing window is 3 minutes, not 5.
  55. The correct answer is D (Loss of myelin impairs saltatory conduction, leaving current to leak and depolarize nodes slowly): Myelin raises membrane resistance and lowers capacitance, so depolarizing current jumps from node to node in saltatory conduction. When myelin is lost, current leaks across the internodal membrane and arrives at the next node weaker and later, slowing or blocking conduction. The resting potential is not abolished. Thicker myelin would speed conduction, and a smaller diameter raises internal resistance rather than lowering it.
  56. The correct answer is D (External intercostal muscles, which elevate the ribs to increase thoracic volume): The external intercostals run obliquely downward and forward, like the hands in a pocket, and elevate the ribs during inspiration to increase thoracic volume. The internal intercostals run downward and backward and depress the ribs in forced expiration. The innermost layer is deepest, separated by the neurovascular bundle. Transversus thoracis lies behind the sternum and depresses the costal cartilages.
  57. The correct answer is A (Three NADH, one FADH2 and one GTP, together with two molecules of carbon dioxide released): One acetyl CoA yields 3 NADH (isocitrate dehydrogenase, alpha-ketoglutarate dehydrogenase, malate dehydrogenase), 1 FADH2 (succinate dehydrogenase) and 1 GTP (succinyl CoA synthetase). Two carbons leave as CO2 in the two decarboxylations. Four NADH and two FADH2 are wrong counts. Three ATP would only arise later from oxidative phosphorylation of the NADH, not as a direct cycle product.
  58. The correct answer is C (The sphenoid bone, which forms part of the cranial base and middle cranial fossa): The neurocranium has eight bones: frontal, two parietals, two temporals, occipital, sphenoid and ethmoid. The sphenoid forms the central floor of the skull and the middle cranial fossa. The zygomatic, maxilla and vomer are among the 14 facial bones, even though they articulate with cranial bones.
  59. The correct answer is B (Obtain the film in a posteroanterior projection with the patient facing the image receptor): Facing the receptor places the breasts and thyroid away from the primary beam, so the PA projection cuts breast dose by a large fraction compared with AP while the curve remains measurable. A waist apron does not shield the chest, and a lower kVp raises absorbed dose. A supine film unloads the spine and can understate the Cobb angle, so standing is kept.
  60. The correct answer is B (No cervical imaging is needed, since she meets low risk criteria for clinical clearance): The NEXUS criteria and the Canadian C-spine rule both allow clinical clearance without imaging when the patient is alert, sober, free of midline tenderness, focal deficit and distracting injury, and can rotate the neck actively. A low speed rear impact is a low risk mechanism, not a dangerous one. A three view series adds radiation with a near zero yield here, and stress films are reserved for suspected ligamentous injury after fracture is excluded. CT is reserved for high risk patients, so it is not justified for this presentation.
  61. They target the rectus abdominis, the lateral wall muscles including quadratus lumborum, and the spinal extensors, using holds of short duration and low compression. Full sit-ups and loaded flexion raise disc load, while flexibility drills and rotational power work do not match the endurance goal.
  62. The plan is rheumatology referral and sacroiliac imaging. A disc herniation would usually give a positive straight leg raise and worsen with sitting. Mechanical sacroiliac and piriformis problems get worse with activity and do not cause prolonged morning stiffness.
  63. It is palpable over the navicular and cuneiform bones. The site behind the medial malleolus is where the posterior tibial pulse is felt, a different vessel. Medial to the tendon or far lateral near the fifth metatarsal misses the artery's usual course.
  64. Mixers add modalities, exercise, and nutrition to adjusting. An evidence-based generalist would use whatever data support, and an osteopathic physician belongs to a different profession with its own training.
  65. The correct answer is A (Low ferritin and serum iron, high total iron-binding capacity and a low mean corpuscular volume): Iron deficiency depletes stores, so ferritin and serum iron fall while the liver makes more transferrin, raising total iron-binding capacity. Reduced hemoglobin synthesis gives small, hypochromic cells. Anemia of chronic disease shows normal or high ferritin with low binding capacity, and overload or sideroblastic states show high ferritin and saturation.
  66. The correct answer is A (Larger resting stroke volume from greater ventricular filling, plus increased vagal tone slowing the sinoatrial node): Cardiac output equals heart rate times stroke volume, so a lower rate with the same output requires a larger stroke volume. Endurance training expands plasma volume and ventricular chamber size, increasing filling and ejected volume, while vagal tone to the sinoatrial node rises. Reduced blood volume or greater sympathetic drive would raise heart rate or lower stroke volume, and the AV node does not set the resting rate.
  67. The correct answer is C (Lyme disease at the early localized stage, diagnosed clinically without waiting for antibody results): An expanding annular erythema migrans lesion after an endemic tick exposure is diagnostic of early localized Lyme disease caused by Borrelia burgdorferi. Serology is often negative in the first weeks, so treatment is started on clinical grounds. Dissemination is marked by multiple lesions, carditis, facial palsy or meningitis, and late disease features arthritis months afterward. Rocky Mountain spotted fever causes a petechial rash beginning on wrists and ankles.
  68. The correct answer is C (Systemic lupus erythematosus, suggested by the rash, photosensitivity and joint involvement together): A malar rash that spares the nasolabial folds, photosensitivity, small joint arthritis and fatigue in a young woman fit systemic lupus erythematosus. Rheumatoid arthritis does not cause a malar rash. Dermatomyositis features a heliotrope rash and proximal weakness, and psoriatic arthritis requires psoriatic skin or nail changes that are absent here.
  69.
