A standard posterolateral herniation would catch the traversing L5 root instead. The S1 root does not pass the L4 to L5 foramen.
  70. The correct answer is A (Pulmonary embolism from deep vein thrombosis, so activate emergency services and keep the patient calm.): Calf swelling after prolonged travel suggests deep vein thrombosis, and pleuritic chest pain with tachycardia and dyspnea suggests that a clot has traveled to the lungs. Pulmonary embolism is an emergency. Costochondritis and viral pleurisy do not explain the swollen calf, and pneumothorax lacks a venous source. Call emergency services and monitor the patient.
  71. The correct answer is A (Ankylosing spondylitis, with thin symmetric marginal syndesmophytes bridging adjacent vertebral bodies): Inflammatory back pain in a young man with thin vertical marginal syndesmophytes and sacroiliac involvement points to ankylosing spondylitis, and the bridging produces the bamboo spine appearance. Psoriatic disease gives thick, asymmetric, nonmarginal outgrowths. Hyperostosis is thick and flowing in an older patient. Degenerative traction spurs do not bridge the disc.
  72. The correct answer is D (The T3 spinous process, roughly level with the root of the spine of the scapula): The root of the spine of the scapula lies at the level of the T3 spinous process, while the inferior angle of the scapula lies at T7. The prominent vertebra at the neck base is C7, with T1 just below it. T12 is used to find the thoracolumbar junction, so these landmarks are different and each is used for counting levels.
  73. The correct answer is C (Steam sterilization in an autoclave at 121 degrees C under pressure for the validated exposure time): Items that contact broken skin or tissue are critical or semicritical and, when heat-stable, should be sterilized rather than only disinfected. Saturated steam at 121 degrees C under pressure denatures proteins and destroys endospores, which are the most resistant forms. Alcohol wiping and quaternary ammonium washing are disinfection steps that do not reliably kill spores. Pasteurization at 70 degrees C reduces vegetative organisms but leaves spores viable.
  74. The correct answer is B (Dihydrofolate reductase is blocked, depleting tetrahydrofolate needed for thymidylate and purine synthesis): Methotrexate is a structural analog of folate that inhibits dihydrofolate reductase and so prevents regeneration of tetrahydrofolate. Tetrahydrofolate carries the one-carbon units used by thymidylate synthase and in purine synthesis, so DNA synthesis stalls. Rapidly dividing cells such as marrow and mucosa are hit hardest, which explains the ulcers and low counts. Fluorouracil, hydroxyurea and allopurinol each act on a different enzyme.
  75. The correct answer is B (Markedly elevated serum alkaline phosphatase with normal serum calcium and normal serum phosphate concentrations): Paget disease of bone is disordered remodeling with excessive osteoclast resorption followed by chaotic osteoblast formation, creating the mosaic pattern. Serum alkaline phosphatase is markedly elevated from osteoblast activity, while calcium and phosphate usually stay normal. Low calcium and phosphate suggest osteomalacia. Hypercalcemia with suppressed parathyroid hormone suggests malignancy, and high parathyroid hormone suggests hyperparathyroidism.
  76. The correct answer is D (All three aponeuroses pass anterior to the muscle, leaving no posterior sheath below the arcuate line): Above the arcuate line the internal oblique splits so that the rectus is enclosed front and back. Below it, the external oblique, internal oblique and transversus abdominis aponeuroses all run in front of the rectus, so the deep surface rests on transversalis fascia. Reversing the direction would leave the muscle exposed under the skin, and a splitting linea alba does not form a sheath at all.
  77. The correct answer is B (Right gluteus medius, the abductor on the stance side): During single leg stance the gluteus medius on the stance side holds the pelvis level. When it is weak, the pelvis drops on the opposite side, so a left drop with right stance means a weak right gluteus medius. A lateral approach can injure its tendon or the superior gluteal nerve. The gluteus maximus and iliopsoas do not control pelvic tilt in stance.
Case N-B06

Patient: M, 67 y, 69 in, 172 lb

Vitals: Pulse 88, resp 18, BP 152/90 mmHg right arm and 148/88 mmHg left arm, temp 98.5 F

Chief complaint and history: Smoker, 45 pack-years, with 2 weeks of deep, constant pain in the low back that wraps to the left flank. Pain is unchanged by rest, position or movement and is not relieved by acetaminophen. He feels full after small meals and has lost 6 lb in 2 months. Hypertension untreated. Father died suddenly at 70.

Examination: Lumbar range of motion full and not painful. No paraspinal spasm or spinal tenderness. Percussion over the left costovertebral angle not painful. Lower extremity strength 5/5. Skin cool over the feet. Left femoral pulse 1+, right 2+. No leg edema.

Orthopedic and neurological tests: Abdomen: expansile pulsatile mass above the umbilicus measuring about 5 cm across, mildly tender, with a systolic bruit. Straight leg raise negative bilaterally. Reflexes 2+ and symmetrical. Sensation intact. Heel and toe walking normal. Kemp test and sacroiliac provocation tests negative.

Laboratory and imaging: Lumbar radiographs, lateral view: curvilinear calcified rim anterior to L2 to L4 about 5.2 cm across, mild L4-5 disc narrowing, no fracture or lytic lesion. Urinalysis normal. Hemoglobin 13.8 g/dL. Fasting glucose 96 mg/dL.

  1. The correct answers are C and D: A pulsatile expansile mass with a bruit and a calcified aortic wall rim on the lateral film identifies an aneurysm, and pain unchanged by motion from a retroperitoneal vascular process is nonmechanical. Disc herniation would produce root signs and position-dependent pain. Pancreatic carcinoma can cause early satiety and back pain, but it does not produce a pulsatile mass. Renal colic is colicky, and the urinalysis is normal.
  2. The correct answers are B and C: An expansile pulsatile mass is the cardinal sign of an abdominal aortic aneurysm, and a bruit with a weaker femoral pulse indicates turbulent flow and reduced distal flow. A negative straight leg raise shows no root tension but would be normal in many other visceral causes. Pain that does not vary with movement points away from a mechanical source but does not name an aneurysm. Elevated blood pressure is a risk factor.
  3. The correct answers are B and E: A tender, symptomatic aneurysm of about 5 cm risks rupture, so imaging and vascular surgical review are needed urgently the same day. Spinal manipulation and deep palpation can raise the risk of rupture. Waiting two weeks for an outpatient study is unsafe in a symptomatic patient. Treating the pain as a strain misses the cause and gives a false sense of safety.
  4. The correct answer is D (Positive screen, so take a fuller alcohol history, apply the AUDIT, and check liver enzymes): Two or more affirmative CAGE answers is a positive screen that suggests problematic drinking, and an eye opener item is especially concerning. The screen is not diagnostic, so the next step is a detailed quantity and frequency history, a validated tool such as the AUDIT, and laboratory review. Detoxification is not decided from a four item screen. Waiting a year ignores a positive result.
  5. The correct answer is C (C7, whose foramen usually carries only vertebral veins and small sympathetic fibers): The vertebral artery usually enters the transverse foramen of C6 and ascends through those of C5 to C1. C7 normally transmits vertebral veins and not the artery, so its foramen is small. The artery passes through the foramina of C2 and C1 before looping behind the atlas. C6 is where the artery enters, not where it is absent.
  6. The correct answer is C (Co-manage: treat the knee in the office while the physician continues managing blood pressure): The knee condition is within chiropractic scope and his blood pressure is controlled under a physician's care, so both providers act at once, which is co-management. Stopping medication to allow care is unsafe and outside scope. Taking over medication advice exceeds scope. Urgent cardiology testing is unwarranted in a patient without cardiac symptoms.
  7. The correct answer is A (Aortic dissection, so call emergency services immediately and avoid manipulation or prolonged examination.): Abrupt tearing pain radiating to the back, uncontrolled hypertension and a blood pressure difference of 20 mmHg or more between the arms strongly suggest aortic dissection, which is immediately life threatening. The doctor calls emergency services and avoids manipulation. An office electrocardiogram or later echocardiogram wastes time, and a mechanical diagnosis does not explain unequal arm pressures.
  8. The correct answer is A (Lost ileal bile acid reabsorption depletes the bile acid pool, reducing micelle formation and fat absorption): Bile acids are reabsorbed by active transport in the terminal ileum and recycled to the liver. Without the ileum, the bile acid pool shrinks, micelles cannot form, and dietary fat passes into the stool as steatorrhea. The low vitamin B12 also reflects ileal loss, because the B12-intrinsic factor complex is absorbed there. Intrinsic factor comes from the stomach and was not removed in this surgery.
  9. The correct answer is A (Stop the current plan and order advanced imaging and laboratory studies for possible malignancy): Age over fifty, night pain, no response to care, and unexplained weight loss are red flags for malignancy such as metastatic disease or myeloma. Manipulative care should stop while MRI and laboratory studies, including a CBC and ESR, are arranged. Plain films can look normal with early marrow disease, and changing technique does not address the underlying concern.
  10. The correct answer is B (Body weight loads the joint, showing true narrowing of the medial joint space): In a standing film the body weight compresses the articular surfaces, so cartilage loss appears as real joint space narrowing, and a supine film can make a narrowed medial compartment look normal. This matters for osteoarthritis grading and for alignment assessment. Exposure factors and dose are not changed by standing. Showing the fibular head is a job for an oblique view, and magnification falls from short object to image distance.
  11. The correct answer is A (Herpes zoster, reactivation of latent varicella virus in the dorsal root ganglion of that nerve): Zoster is reactivation of varicella virus from a sensory ganglion, so the eruption is unilateral, painful and confined to one dermatome, and burning pain often precedes the vesicles by days. Herpes simplex recurs at a fixed site and does not follow a thoracic dermatome. Poison ivy itches rather than burns and has no dermatomal border. Bullous pemphigoid blisters are tense, widespread and seen mostly in the elderly.
  12. The correct answer is D (Foraminal narrowing with nerve root compression, since traction widens the intervertebral foramen): Cervical distraction lifts the head and enlarges the intervertebral foramina, so arm pain that eases implies a compressed root. Ligament sprain and muscle strain would not improve and often feel worse when the neck is stretched. Raised intrathecal pressure belongs to the Valsalva maneuver, which asks the patient to bear down rather than receive traction. A positive distraction test supports a radicular source and favors foraminal compression testing results.
  13. The correct answer is D (Swimmer's lateral, with one arm raised overhead and the other depressed to show C7-T1): The swimmer's (Twining) lateral separates the shoulder from the cervicothoracic junction by raising one arm and depressing the other, so C7 and T1 are seen in profile. In trauma, the study is incomplete until C7-T1 is visualized, so a repeat or alternative view is required. The open mouth view shows the atlas and dens, the pillar view shows facet joints, and the oblique view shows foramina, so none of them solves a missing C7-T1 on the lateral.
  14. The correct answer is A (Dorsal rami of the spinal nerves at the corresponding vertebral segments): The true or intrinsic back muscles, including the erector spinae, are supplied segmentally by the dorsal rami of the spinal nerves. Ventral rami supply the limbs and the anterior and lateral trunk wall. The thoracodorsal nerve supplies only latissimus dorsi, an extrinsic muscle, and the spinal accessory nerve supplies trapezius and sternocleidomastoid.
  15. The correct answer is B (Chronic granulomatous inflammation with caseous necrosis, sustained by CD4 T-cell activation of macrophages): Epithelioid macrophages with Langhans giant cells and central caseous necrosis define a caseating granuloma, the classic reaction to Mycobacterium tuberculosis. Interferon gamma from CD4 T cells activates macrophages. Sarcoidosis granulomas are noncaseating, abscesses are neutrophil rich, and fibrinous exudates form on serosal surfaces.
  16. The correct answer is C (Wolff law, where bone remodels its architecture and density to match the stresses placed upon it): Wolff law states that bone adapts to the loads it bears, so chronically overloaded segments show sclerosis and osteophyte formation along stress lines. Davis law concerns soft tissue, Hilton law describes nerve supply, and Hueter-Volkmann applies to growth plates in children, not to this mature spine.
  17. The correct answer is C (Acute pancreatitis, driven by alcohol-related inflammation of the pancreas): Epigastric pain boring through to the back, eased by leaning forward, with vomiting and a long history of heavy alcohol use is the classic presentation of acute pancreatitis. Perforation causes rigidity and free air, cholecystitis localizes to the right upper quadrant with a Murphy sign, and an aneurysm gives a pulsatile mass without postural relief. Serum lipase confirms the diagnosis.
  18. The correct answer is B (Acute uncomplicated cystitis, most often caused by Escherichia coli): Positive nitrite, 3+ leukocyte esterase, 40 white cells per field and many bacteria with dysuria and no systemic signs indicate lower urinary tract infection. Escherichia coli causes most uncomplicated cases because it reduces nitrate to nitrite. Chlamydial urethritis gives pyuria without nitrite or bacteria. Glomerulonephritis shows red cells and casts, and candida contamination would show yeast.
  19. The correct answer is B (Neurocysticercosis, from ingesting Taenia solium eggs that develop into larval cysts in the brain): Taenia solium eggs shed by a human tapeworm carrier are swallowed, and the larvae form cysts in brain tissue, with the scolex appearing as a central dot on MRI. This is a leading cause of adult-onset seizures in endemic regions. Toxoplasmosis produces ring enhancing lesions in immunosuppressed hosts, echinococcosis forms a large hepatic hydatid cyst, and trichinellosis encysts in skeletal muscle.
  20. The correct answer is B (Undigested lactose remains in the lumen, drawing water in osmotically as bacteria ferment it to gas): Lactase on the brush border splits lactose into glucose and galactose. When it is deficient, lactose remains in the gut lumen, where it is osmotically active and pulls water into the bowel. Colonic bacteria ferment it to organic acids and hydrogen, explaining the acidic stool, gas and positive breath test. Secretory and allergic mechanisms do not produce this pattern.
  21. The correct answer is C (Withhold adjusting and arrange urgent advanced imaging, notifying her oncologist or physician): Prior breast cancer with constant night pain, weight loss and focal spinal tenderness is suspicious for spinal metastasis. Manipulation could cause a pathologic fracture, so it is withheld while urgent MRI or comparable advanced imaging is arranged and her physician is told. Plain films can look normal early, and waiting for neurologic loss delays treatment of impending cord compression.
  22. The correct answer is A (Testicular torsion, requiring immediate emergency transfer for surgical exploration): Sudden severe scrotal pain with vomiting, a high-riding transverse testis and an absent cremasteric reflex is testicular torsion, and testicular salvage falls sharply after about six hours. The patient needs immediate emergency transfer for surgery. Epididymitis develops over days with a preserved reflex, appendage torsion keeps the reflex intact, and a hernia would not explain the testicular position.
  23. The correct answer is C (Right rotation, to the same side as the side bending, because the segment is non-neutral): Fryette's second principle states that when a lumbar or thoracic segment is flexed or extended, side bending and rotation occur to the same side. The first principle, with opposite-side coupling, applies only to the neutral position and group curves. Flexion and extension give the same coupling direction, and the facets are unloaded rather than locked in flexion.
  24. The correct answer is A (Withhold manipulation and arrange emergency transfer for urgent MRI and surgical evaluation): Saddle anesthesia, urinary retention, reduced rectal tone and bilateral sciatica make cauda equina syndrome the working diagnosis. The nerve roots need surgical decompression, ideally within 24 to 48 hours, to prevent permanent bladder, bowel and sexual dysfunction. Any manipulation or traction can worsen compression, and waiting weeks for an outpatient scan is unsafe.
  25. The correct answer is C (Osteoporotic compression fracture with anterior wedging of the twelfth thoracic body): Minor trauma in a thin, older, postmenopausal woman, with a single anterior wedge deformity at the thoracolumbar junction and diffuse osteopenia, is the typical osteoporotic compression fracture. A pathologic fracture would show cortical destruction or a pedicle lesion, Scheuermann disease starts in adolescence with several wedged bodies, and a burst fracture needs high energy and retropulsion. DEXA and treatment follow.
  26. The correct answer is A (Fibromyalgia, a chronic widespread pain disorder with central sensitization and sleep disturbance): Widespread pain lasting over three months, unrefreshing sleep, fatigue, cognitive complaints and normal laboratory tests describe fibromyalgia. Central sensitization of pain is thought to underlie it. Polymyalgia rheumatica would raise the ESR and affect patients over 50. Normal TSH and creatine kinase argue against hypothyroid myopathy, and lupus usually leaves a serologic trace.
  27. The correct answer is C (Maternal alcohol consumption during pregnancy, which disrupts neural crest cell survival and brain development): Fetal alcohol syndrome produces growth restriction, microcephaly, short palpebral fissures, a smooth philtrum, a thin upper lip, and neurodevelopmental deficits. Alcohol is the leading preventable cause of intellectual disability. Phenytoin embryopathy adds nail and distal phalangeal hypoplasia, and isotretinoin causes ear, cardiac, and thymic malformations. Cocaine is linked mainly to abruption and growth restriction rather than this facial pattern.
  28. The correct answer is D (Abnormal, exceeding the 3 mm adult limit, and indicating transverse ligament laxity with cord risk): The adult atlantodental interval should not exceed about 3 mm, and a larger value in flexion suggests transverse ligament laxity, a common cause in rheumatoid arthritis through pannus erosion. Instability puts the spinal cord at risk and calls for caution and further evaluation. The 5 mm limit applies to children, and neither a dens fracture nor os odontoideum is diagnosed by this interval alone.
  29. The correct answer is D (Metastatic prostate cancer with osteoblastic spinal lesions, so arrange bone scan and oncology referral): A PSA of 48 ng/mL is far above levels seen in benign enlargement, and a high ALP with night pain in the thoracic spine indicates osteoblastic bone metastasis, the usual pattern of prostate cancer. Staging with bone scan and urgent oncology referral is required, and manipulation of the region is contraindicated. Myeloma gives lytic lesions with hypercalcemia and normal ALP, and Paget disease does not raise PSA.
  30. The correct answer is B (Sacral parasympathetic efferents contract the detrusor, while the pudendal nerve controls the external sphincter voluntarily): Pelvic splanchnic parasympathetic fibers from S2 to S4 contract the detrusor to void. The external urethral sphincter is skeletal muscle supplied by the somatic pudendal nerve, so it can be held closed voluntarily. Sympathetic input promotes storage by relaxing the detrusor and closing the internal sphincter. Parasympathetic input does not relax the detrusor, and the pudendal nerve does not drive it.
  31. The correct answer is A (Varicocele, left sided because the testicular vein drains into the renal vein): Dilated pampiniform veins feel like a bag of worms, enlarge on standing and shrink when supine. The left testicular vein enters the left renal vein at a right angle and is longer, which raises pressure and favors the left side. The right testicular vein drains directly into the inferior vena cava. Spermatoceles and hydroceles are cystic and fluid filled, and they do not empty on lying down.
  32. The correct answer is C (Kawasaki disease, with coronary artery aneurysm as the major complication, treated with immunoglobulin and aspirin): Fever for five days or more with conjunctivitis, oral changes, rash, extremity swelling and cervical lymphadenopathy is Kawasaki disease, a vasculitis of medium vessels. The feared complication is coronary artery aneurysm, which intravenous immunoglobulin given early reduces. Scarlet fever, measles and mononucleosis each have different features and complications.
  33. The correct answer is A (It lowers activation energy so equilibrium is reached sooner, without moving the equilibrium): Enzymes are catalysts that stabilize the transition state, lowering activation energy and speeding the approach to equilibrium. They do not change the free energy difference between reactants and products, so the equilibrium constant and the direction of the reaction are unchanged. Enzymes do not donate energy or make a nonspontaneous reaction proceed, and they emerge unchanged and are reused.
  34. The correct answer is D (Lower the screen so its top sits at or below eye level, centered directly ahead): Neutral neck posture needs the top of the screen at or slightly below eye level and directly in front of the user, which removes both the sustained neck extension and the sustained rotation. Raising the screen or leaving it off center keeps those loads in place. A footrest or longer viewing distance may help comfort but does not fix the head position causing her pain.
  35. The correct answer is C (Long-term glucocorticoid use lowers bone density and raises the risk of vertebral compression fracture): Daily prednisone for years causes glucocorticoid-induced osteoporosis, and a minor flexion load can produce a vertebral compression fracture with focal percussion tenderness. That makes the steroid history the key past history item. Cough strain is possible but does not explain focal tenderness at T8, steroids can mask infection rather than reduce its risk, and adrenal suppression does not produce referred thoracic pain.
  36. The correct answer is C (Deep fibular nerve, a branch of the common fibular nerve): The anterior compartment muscles, which include tibialis anterior, extensor hallucis longus and extensor digitorum longus, dorsiflex the foot and are supplied by the deep fibular nerve. The superficial fibular nerve supplies the lateral compartment evertors. The tibial nerve supplies the posterior compartment plantar flexors. The saphenous nerve is sensory and gives no motor supply to the leg.
  37. The correct answer is D (Juvenile idiopathic arthritis, with epiphyseal overgrowth from chronic hyperemia and a squared patella): Chronic synovial hyperemia stimulates the growth plates, so the epiphyses enlarge, the intercondylar notch widens and the inferior patella becomes squared. Hemophilia produces the same knee pattern, but this girl has no bleeding history, and chronic eye inflammation points to juvenile idiopathic arthritis. Septic arthritis destroys joint space rapidly, and osteochondritis dissecans is a focal condylar defect.
  38. The correct answer is D (Flexion-distraction on a table with a movable caudal section, using gentle intermittent traction and flexion): Flexion-distraction, as taught in the Cox method, uses gentle table-assisted traction and flexion to unload the disc and suits a patient who cannot tolerate side posture. The roll is the side posture procedure the patient cannot tolerate, extension thrusts load the posterior annulus, and sustained rotation adds shear to a herniated disc.
  39. The correct answer is B (Ankylosing spondylitis, with thin marginal syndesmophytes and bilateral sacroiliac fusion): Ankylosing spondylitis in a young man produces thin, symmetric, marginal syndesmophytes that give a bamboo contour, with early and bilateral sacroiliac disease that progresses to fusion. DISH has thick, flowing ossification, appears in older patients, and spares the sacroiliac joints. Psoriatic arthritis gives asymmetric, bulky nonmarginal bridging. Osteoarthritis produces osteophytes and facet changes without continuous bridging.
Case N-B16

Patient: F, 47 y, 64 in, 181 lb

Vitals: Pulse 74, resp 16, BP 124/80 mmHg, temp 98.1 F

Chief complaint and history: Hospital nurse on 12-hour shifts on hard floors. Three months of right heel pain, sharpest with the first steps out of bed and after sitting, easing after about 5 minutes of walking and returning by evening. Pain 7/10 at first step. No night pain at rest, no trauma, no numbness. BMI 31. Wears worn cushioned clogs.

Examination: Right heel tender at the medial calcaneal tubercle. Midfoot and Achilles insertion nontender. Medial and lateral calcaneal squeeze painless. Arch height normal sitting and lowers on standing. Right gastrocnemius tight. No heel swelling or warmth.

Orthopedic and neurological tests: Windlass test positive on the right, heel pain with passive great toe extension. Weight bearing dorsiflexion, knee extended: right 4 degrees, left 10 degrees. Tinel behind the medial malleolus negative. Anterior drawer stable. Single heel raise 10 repetitions. Sensation and reflexes normal.

Laboratory and imaging: No imaging done. Fasting glucose 98 mg/dL, uric acid 5.1 mg/dL. No inflammatory arthritis, no back pain, no morning stiffness elsewhere.

  1. The correct answers are A and E: First-step pain that eases with walking is the classic plantar fasciitis history, and tenderness at the medial calcaneal tubercle with a painless squeeze points to the fascial origin and away from a calcaneal stress fracture. Body mass, standing hours and worn shoes raise risk but do not localize the lesion. Normal glucose and uric acid only make metabolic causes less likely.
  2. The correct answers are A and C: The windlass test tensions the plantar fascia by extending the great toe, so heel pain reproduction supports the fascia as the source. Reduced weight bearing dorsiflexion with the knee extended reflects gastrocnemius tightness, a recognized contributor. In this chart the tibial Tinel, the calcaneal squeeze and the drawer test are negative or painless, which argues against tarsal tunnel, stress fracture and ligament sprain.
  3. The correct answers are B and E: Calf and plantar fascia stretching, taping or an arch support, and better footwear form the evidence-based first-line plan. Imaging is reserved for persistent pain after an adherent trial or when red flags appear. Heel spurs are frequent in asymptomatic feet, so a radiograph at the first visit does not change care. Injection and surgery are later options.
  4. The correct answer is D (Cervical facets lie about 45 degrees to the transverse plane, thoracic about 60, lumbar near sagittal): Cervical facets face up and back at about 45 degrees to the transverse plane, thoracic facets are steeper at about 60 degrees with a coronal tilt, and lumbar facets are oriented near the sagittal plane, which favors flexion and extension and limits rotation. The other options reverse or mix these regional values.
  5. The correct answer is D (A lesion of the upper motor neuron corticospinal pathway): An extensor great toe with fanning of the other toes is the Babinski sign, which indicates loss of corticospinal inhibition from an upper motor neuron lesion. In a healthy adult the toes flex. A root lesion would cause weakness and reflex loss rather than an extensor response. Cerebellar disease affects coordination, not plantar reflex direction.
  6. The correct answer is B (Between days three and five, when the proliferative phase builds new vascularized connective tissue): Granulation tissue, with fibroblasts and new capillaries, typically appears around days three to five after injury. The tissue is pink and granular and supports later collagen deposition. In the first day the wound shows neutrophils and fibrin with no organized fibroblast response. By weeks two to three type I collagen replaces the early matrix, and tensile strength continues rising for months.
  7. The correct answer is D (Peritubular interstitial fibroblasts of the kidney, stimulated by reduced oxygen delivery to the renal cortex): Erythropoietin is made mainly by peritubular interstitial cells of the renal cortex, and hypoxia inducible factor increases its transcription when oxygen delivery falls. The hormone stimulates erythroid precursors in bone marrow, raising hematocrit. Juxtaglomerular cells secrete renin in response to low perfusion. Megakaryocytes respond to thrombopoietin, and the adult liver is only a minor source.
  8. The correct answer is D (Reassortment of the segmented RNA genome in a co-infected host cell, producing a major antigenic shift): Influenza A has eight RNA segments, so a cell infected by two strains can package a mixed set, a process called reassortment. A sudden new hemagglutinin from this swap is antigenic shift, to which the population has no immunity. Gradual point mutations produce antigenic drift, which explains yearly epidemics. Provirus integration belongs to retroviruses, and phase variation is a bacterial mechanism.
  9. The correct answer is A (Acknowledge the worry, state plainly that a same day evaluation is needed, and explain next steps.): Effective bad news communication pairs empathy with clear, honest, plain language: name the emotion, say why the evaluation is needed today, and describe the next steps. False reassurance and vague timing weaken urgency, a list of technical terms increases fear, and leaving a letter without a conversation abandons the patient. This earns Interpersonal and Communication credit.

Practice Test 5 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is C (Order advanced imaging and arrange oncology evaluation first, withholding manipulation until metastasis is excluded): Prior cancer, age over 50, night pain, and unexplained weight loss are classic malignancy red flags, and bone metastasis from prostate cancer is likely. Advanced imaging and oncology evaluation come before any manipulation. Plain films miss early lesions. A two-week trial delays diagnosis. Weight loss in this setting is a symptom to investigate, not a diet issue.
  2. The correct answer is D (Nerve root irritation within the intervertebral foramen, consistent with cervical radiculopathy): Distraction widens the foramen and takes pressure off the irritated root, while compression and lateral flexion narrow it and reproduce the symptoms, which is the classic radicular pattern. The thumb and index finger distribution fits the C6 root. Rotator cuff pain does not change with cervical traction or compression. Thoracic outlet syndrome is provoked by arm elevation and shoulder position, and central cord syndrome would present with bilateral hand deficits.
  3. The correct answer is A (Alcohol-related liver injury, shown by an AST to ALT ratio above 2 with disproportionate GGT elevation): An AST to ALT ratio of about 2 to 1 (168 to 82), with modest transaminase levels below 300, a markedly raised GGT and macrocytosis (MCV 103) is characteristic of alcohol-related liver disease. The low albumin and prolonged INR show impaired synthetic function. Viral hepatitis produces higher ALT than AST. Obstruction would give much higher ALP and conjugated bilirubin, and fatty liver disease has ALT above AST.
  4. The correct answer is B (Vein, artery, then nerve, from superior to inferior in the costal groove): The costal groove holds the intercostal vein most superiorly, the artery in the middle and the nerve inferiorly, remembered as VAN from top to bottom. The needle is therefore passed along the upper border of the rib below the space, which keeps it away from the bundle that is tucked under the rib above. At the midaxillary line the collateral branches are small, so risk is lowest there.
  5. The correct answer is B (Pneumothorax, with a thin visceral pleural line and absent lung markings beyond it): A pneumothorax shows a thin visceral pleural line with no lung markings peripheral to it, and it is common in tall thin young men with spontaneous rupture of an apical bleb. Effusions layer at the base with a meniscus. Lobar pneumonia is dense with air bronchograms. A giant bulla is a thin-walled air space but its edge curves toward the lung, unlike the lung edge in a pneumothorax.
  6. The correct answer is B (Ankylosing spondylitis, supported by HLA-B27 positivity and sacroiliac joint changes on imaging): Young age, insidious onset, prolonged morning stiffness, improvement with exercise, and uveitis define inflammatory back pain from axial spondyloarthritis. Ankylosing spondylitis is associated with HLA-B27 and sacroiliitis. Disc pain usually improves with rest. Rheumatoid arthritis spares the sacroiliac joints, and DISH affects older people without inflammatory pain or sacroiliitis.
  7. The correct answer is C (Acute hepatitis B, since surface antigen shows active infection and IgM core antibody marks recent exposure): Surface antigen shows the virus is present, and IgM anti-HBc is the marker of recent infection, so together they indicate acute disease. Chronic infection shows persistent surface antigen beyond six months with IgG rather than IgM core antibody. Vaccination yields only anti-HBs, with no antigen and no core antibody. Resolved infection shows negative surface antigen with positive anti-HBs and anti-HBc IgG.
  8. The correct answer is B (Degenerative disc disease, because intradiscal gas and intact endplates argue against discitis): Narrowing at one disc with marginal osteophytes in an older patient is degenerative disc disease. The vacuum phenomenon, gas inside the disc, indicates a degenerated disc, and infection destroys the endplates early, so intact endplates and gas argue against discitis. DISH preserves disc height, and ankylosing spondylitis starts in the sacroiliac joints with thin syndesmophytes.
  9. The correct answer is B (Glucagon from alpha cells, stimulating hepatic glycogenolysis and gluconeogenesis to release glucose): During fasting, insulin falls and glucagon rises, and glucagon acts on the liver to break down glycogen and make new glucose from amino acids, lactate and glycerol. Insulin promotes storage, which is the fed state. Muscle glycogen cannot release free glucose because muscle lacks glucose 6 phosphatase, and GLP-1 increases rather than decreases insulin secretion.
Case N-B12

Patient: M, 71 y, 69 in, 158 lb

Vitals: Pulse 74, resp 16, BP 140/84 mmHg, temp 98.6 F

Chief complaint and history: Retired mechanic with 2 months of progressive low back pain that is constant and worse lying down at night. Lost 14 lb without dieting. Rises 3 to 4 times nightly to urinate with a weak stream. No leg weakness or incontinence. Pain not relieved by NSAIDs or rest. No prior cancer. Father died of colon cancer. Never had a PSA test.

Examination: Thin and mildly pale. Lumbar flexion and extension limited by pain. Percussion tenderness at L3 and L4. Abdomen soft, bladder not palpable. Inguinal nodes not enlarged. Rectal examination: prostate enlarged, hard and irregular with loss of the median sulcus. No rectal mass.

Orthopedic and neurological tests: Straight leg raise and femoral stretch negative bilaterally. Lower limb strength 5/5, reflexes 2+, plantar responses flexor. Perianal sensation intact and rectal tone normal. Gait normal. Kemp test causes local midline pain without radiation.

Laboratory and imaging: Lumbar films not yet taken. Urinalysis: trace blood, no nitrite, no leukocyte esterase. Hemoglobin 11.9 g/dL. Creatinine 1.1 mg/dL. Calcium 9.4 mg/dL. Prostate specific antigen has not been measured.

  1. The correct answers are B and C: Constant pain that is not eased by rest, unexplained weight loss and a hard, irregular, asymmetric prostate are red flags for prostate cancer with bone spread. Percussion tenderness occurs with fractures, infection and tumor as well as other conditions. Nocturia with weak stream is common with benign enlargement but does not explain night pain with weight loss. Metastases may exist without root signs because bone is involved before nerves.
  2. The correct answers are B and C: A hard, irregular prostate with constitutional symptoms and constant, unremitting spinal pain points to prostate carcinoma spreading to bone, and anemia with weight loss reflects systemic disease. Benign hyperplasia produces a smooth, firm gland and no weight loss. Myeloma typically raises calcium and creatinine, which are normal here. Stenosis causes leg symptoms on walking that ease with sitting, which are absent.
  3. The correct answers are A and D: Serum PSA is markedly elevated with metastatic prostate cancer and is the key supporting laboratory test. Prostate metastases are typically osteoblastic, so films show dense sclerotic vertebrae, often with a raised alkaline phosphatase. Protein electrophoresis and punched out lytic lesions belong to myeloma. A urine culture would not explain the pain because the urinalysis shows no infection.
  4. The correct answer is D (Bone marrow and the intestinal mucosa because both contain rapidly dividing precursor cells): Ionizing radiation damages DNA, and cells that divide rapidly or are undifferentiated cannot repair before mitosis. Bone marrow precursors and intestinal crypt cells therefore show early injury, causing cytopenias and diarrhea. Neurons and cardiac myocytes are postmitotic and relatively resistant. Cartilage, cortical bone, muscle, and hepatocytes divide slowly and are less sensitive.
  5. The correct answer is A (Perineal numbness with urinary retention and weakness in both legs, needing same day emergency referral.): Saddle numbness, new urinary retention and bilateral leg weakness describe cauda equina syndrome, a surgical emergency that needs same day imaging and decompression. A single root pattern with a positive straight leg raise is managed conservatively at first. Morning stiffness with reduced chest expansion suggests ankylosing spondylitis, and a spasm after a minor fall without neurologic deficit is a mechanical strain.
  6. The correct answer is D (L4-L5 disc herniation compressing the traversing L5 nerve root): Weak dorsiflexion, impaired heel walking and numbness on the top of the foot indicate the L5 root. The most common cause is a posterolateral L4-L5 herniation, which compresses the traversing L5 root. The L5-S1 and L3-L4 levels would involve the S1 and L4 roots with different reflex and motor findings. A foraminal L5-S1 lesion can affect the exiting L5 root, but a lifting injury most often produces the posterolateral pattern.
  7. The correct answer is D (The sinuvertebral nerve, a recurrent branch of the ventral ramus joined by sympathetic fibers): The sinuvertebral or recurrent meningeal nerve arises near the ventral ramus, receives a sympathetic contribution, and re-enters the vertebral canal through the foramen. It supplies the outer third of the annulus, the posterior longitudinal ligament and the ventral dura, so annular tears are a source of discogenic pain. The medial branch of the dorsal ramus supplies the facet joint. Dorsal ramus lateral branches serve muscle and skin, not the disc.
  8. The correct answer is B (Refer urgently for venous duplex ultrasonography and anticoagulation assessment, avoiding massage of the leg): Unilateral calf swelling over 3 cm, warmth, tenderness and recent prolonged immobility give a high Wells score for deep vein thrombosis. The standard next step is prompt duplex ultrasound and assessment for anticoagulation. Massage or manipulation could dislodge a thrombus and cause pulmonary embolism. A delay of a week for conservative care is unsafe, and spinal imaging does not address a venous cause.
  9. The correct answer is C (Direct inguinal hernia, passing through the inguinal triangle bordered by the rectus sheath and inguinal ligament): A direct inguinal hernia bulges through the weakened posterior wall of the canal within the inguinal triangle, which is bounded by the inferior epigastric vessels, the lateral border of rectus abdominis and the inguinal ligament. It lies medial to the inferior epigastric vessels. An indirect hernia enters at the deep ring lateral to them. A femoral hernia lies below the ligament, and an obturator hernia exits through the obturator canal.
  10. The correct answer is D (Keep vitamin K intake steady week to week and tell the prescriber before major diet changes): Warfarin works by blocking vitamin K dependent clotting factors, so sudden swings in vitamin K intake move the INR. The practical rule is consistency in leafy green intake, with the prescriber informed before large changes so the dose and INR checks can be adjusted. Eliminating greens or adding supplements changes intake just as much, and the dose never adjusts on its own.
  11. The correct answer is D (Anti-double stranded DNA antibody, which is highly specific and tracks renal involvement): Systemic lupus erythematosus is suggested by the malar rash, photosensitivity, arthralgias and proteinuria. Antinuclear antibody is highly sensitive but not specific, so a positive result needs confirmation. Anti-double stranded DNA and anti-Smith antibodies are the specific markers, and anti-double stranded DNA also parallels lupus nephritis activity. Antihistone antibodies suggest drug induced lupus and anticentromere antibodies suggest limited scleroderma.
  12. The correct answer is C (Gout, with punched-out erosions bearing overhanging edges and preserved bone density and joint space): Acute nocturnal great toe pain in a middle aged man, with punched-out erosions that have overhanging edges, a tophus, preserved joint space, and normal bone density, is typical gout. The edge that overhangs the erosion and the lack of osteopenia separate it from rheumatoid arthritis. Osteoarthritis does not erode, and psoriatic arthritis adds periostitis and a pencil-in-cup change.
  13. The correct answer is D (Inject epinephrine 0.3 to 0.5 mg intramuscularly into the anterolateral thigh, then lay her supine): Hives, airway swelling, wheezing and hypotension after an exposure are anaphylaxis, and intramuscular epinephrine into the anterolateral thigh is the first drug. Absorption there is faster and more reliable than subcutaneous or deltoid injection. Antihistamines and albuterol are adjuncts and must not delay epinephrine. Laying her supine with legs raised supports blood pressure.
  14. The correct answer is B (Vitamins A, D, E and K, which are absorbed in micelles together with dietary fat): The fat-soluble vitamins are A, D, E and K, and they need bile salts, pancreatic lipase and micelle formation to be absorbed. In cystic fibrosis, thick secretions block pancreatic ducts, so fat digestion is poor and these vitamins are lost in the stool. Vitamin C, B6, B12 and folate are water soluble and enter the body without micelles. That is why routine supplementation includes A, D, E and K.
  15. The correct answer is C (C8, which exits through the C7 to T1 foramen below the C7 vertebra): The C8 root exits through the C7 to T1 foramen and supplies the little finger and the hand intrinsics, including finger abduction. T1 would also weaken the intrinsics but produces sensory loss in the medial arm, which is normal here. C7 serves the triceps and middle finger, and C6 serves the thumb and biceps.
  16. The correct answer is C (Bilateral basilar crackles persisting after cough, recorded under Objective with location and sides noted): Brief popping sounds that persist after a cough are crackles, and basilar crackles are an examiner finding that belongs under Objective with location and side. Wheezes are continuous and musical, so they do not fit the sound described, and a patient report alone is Subjective. A friction rub is a grating sound tied to both phases of breathing, and bronchial breath sounds over the bases would be a different finding. A diagnosis such as pneumonia belongs in the Assessment only after the findings are weighed, not as the auscultation entry.
  17. The correct answer is B (Cellular swelling and fatty change, because membranes and nuclei remain intact if oxygen returns): Failure of the sodium pump lets sodium and water enter the cell, causing swelling, and impaired lipid metabolism produces fatty change. These are reversible if oxygen is restored. Karyolysis, mitochondrial amorphous densities and plasma membrane rupture show irreversible injury and necrosis.
  18. The correct answer is A (The anteroposterior open mouth view, with the central ray through the open mouth): The open mouth view is taken with the mouth open so the teeth and skull base do not cover the upper cervical spine, which lets the central ray show the dens, C1 lateral masses, and the atlantoaxial joints. The lateral view shows alignment and soft tissue but not the lateral masses. The lower cervical view and the oblique views examine other levels and the foramina.
  19. The correct answer is A (Common fibular nerve, injured as it winds around the neck of the fibula): The common fibular nerve is superficial at the fibular neck and supplies the dorsiflexors, the evertors and the skin of the dorsal foot, so injury there causes foot drop and steppage gait. The tibial nerve supplies the plantar flexors and invertors, which are spared. The superior gluteal nerve would cause a Trendelenburg pattern rather than foot slap. The femoral nerve supplies the quadriceps and would impair knee extension, not dorsiflexion.
  20. The correct answer is D (Inferior mesenteric artery, which supplies the hindgut from the distal transverse colon to the upper rectum): The inferior mesenteric artery arises from the aorta at L3 and supplies the hindgut, from the distal transverse colon through the descending and sigmoid colon to the upper rectum. It is often ligated or occluded during aneurysm repair, producing left colon ischemia. The superior mesenteric artery supplies the midgut. The celiac trunk supplies the foregut, and the internal iliac supplies pelvic structures rather than the descending colon.
  21. The correct answer is B (To identify which segments need a thrust by noting a change in the relative leg length): The Activator protocol uses the apparent change in relative leg length during isolation tests to find segments considered dysfunctional and in need of an instrument thrust. It is a functional indicator, not a measure of true leg length, laxity or the force to be delivered. The instrument's force setting is a separate selection made by the doctor.
  22. The correct answer is C (Active force is low because few myosin heads can bind actin at minimal filament overlap): Active tension depends on the number of cross-bridges formed, which is greatest near optimal sarcomere length where thick and thin filaments overlap fully. When the fiber is overstretched, overlap is minimal and few myosin heads reach actin, so force is low. Filament collision and double overlap belong to markedly shortened sarcomeres. Calcium release is similar, but force still varies with length because overlap changes.
  23. The correct answer is B (Paget disease, with a markedly raised serum alkaline phosphatase): Paget disease thickens the calvarium and produces patchy cotton-wool sclerosis, and it often involves the tibia with bowing and the temporal bone with hearing loss. Rapid bone turnover markedly raises alkaline phosphatase while calcium and phosphorus stay normal. Hyperparathyroidism gives a salt and pepper skull, and myeloma produces punched-out lytic defects.
  24. The correct answer is D (Femoral vein, lying within the femoral sheath medial to the artery): From lateral to medial the femoral triangle contains the nerve, artery, vein and lymphatics, remembered as NAVEL. The femoral vein lies immediately medial to the artery within the femoral sheath. The femoral nerve is lateral and outside the sheath, and the great saphenous vein joins the femoral vein below the inguinal ligament.
  25. The correct answer is A (The transverse ligament of the atlas, which crosses behind the dens): The transverse ligament stretches between the lateral masses of the atlas and presses the dens against the facet on the anterior arch, preventing forward slide of the atlas. The alar ligaments check rotation and side bending, the apical ligament is a minor vestige and the tectorial membrane lies behind both, as the upward continuation of the posterior longitudinal ligament.
  26. The correct answer is D (Acute gouty arthritis from monosodium urate crystal deposition in the first metatarsophalangeal joint): Abrupt onset at night in the first metatarsophalangeal joint after purines and alcohol is the classic pattern of gout. Monosodium urate crystals trigger the inflammation, and a low grade fever can occur. Septic arthritis must be considered, but the trigger and site favor gout. Pseudogout favors the knee and wrist, and hallux rigidus is a slow degenerative process.
  27. The correct answer is A (Lipid A of lipopolysaccharide in the outer membrane, which binds toll like receptor 4 on macrophages): Endotoxin is the lipid A portion of lipopolysaccharide embedded in the gram negative outer membrane, and it is released when bacteria die. It binds toll like receptor 4 and triggers interleukin 1, interleukin 6, and tumor necrosis factor release, causing fever and septic shock. The O antigen is immunogenic but not toxic. Teichoic acids belong to gram positive bacteria, and a heat labile exotoxin is secreted by living cells.
  28. The correct answer is A (Add a dated, signed addendum giving the correct side and leave the original entry legible): A chart correction must keep the original entry readable and add a dated, signed addendum explaining the change, so the record shows what was written and when it was corrected. Erasing or covering the entry hides the original and can look like tampering. Replacing the page destroys part of the legal record and backdates a new note. A verbal comment to the patient leaves the written record wrong.
  29. The correct answer is D (Explain the planned examination, obtain verbal consent, and offer a gown or drape before undressing.): Part IV graders expect the doctor to say what will be done, ask permission, and protect the patient's privacy with a gown or drape before any undressing or contact. Explaining after the patient has changed removes the chance to decline. Palpating through clothing first or relying on a front desk form skips the doctor's own explanation and consent. Doing this aloud also earns Interpersonal credit on video.
  30. The correct answer is D (The plateau from L-type calcium entry holds the refractory period nearly as long as contraction): Ventricular myocytes have a long plateau phase sustained by inward L-type calcium current, so the absolute refractory period lasts nearly the entire contraction. A second stimulus therefore cannot trigger another action potential until the muscle is relaxing, which prevents summation and tetany. Skeletal fibers have brief action potentials, so twitches can summate. The sinoatrial node paces the heart but does not restrict individual myocyte stimulation.
  31. The correct answer is C (Oral leukoplakia, with incisional biopsy to exclude dysplasia or squamous cell carcinoma): A white plaque that cannot be scraped off and cannot be attributed to another disease is leukoplakia, and the lateral tongue in a smoker is a high-risk site for dysplasia or carcinoma. Biopsy is the next step. Candidiasis wipes off and responds to antifungals. Lichen planus is usually bilateral and lacy, and geographic tongue changes pattern over days.
  32. The correct answer is C (Sensory loss in the median distribution that splits the ring finger, sparing thenar eminence skin): The palmar cutaneous branch of the median nerve leaves before the carpal tunnel, so sensation over the thenar eminence is normal in carpal tunnel syndrome. The ring finger is split, with the median side affected and the ulnar side spared. Cervical radiculopathy would involve the thenar skin and change with neck position. A reduced brachioradialis reflex and wrist extensor weakness point to a C6 root.
  33. The correct answer is D (The inferior gluteal nerve, supplying the gluteus maximus muscle): The inferior gluteal nerve supplies the gluteus maximus, the main hip extensor needed to rise from a chair and climb stairs. Normal abduction argues against the superior gluteal nerve, which would give a Trendelenburg sign. The obturator nerve produces weak adduction and the femoral nerve weak knee extension, not weak hip extension.
  34. The correct answer is D (The anterior spinal artery, which supplies the anterior two thirds of the spinal cord): Anterior cord syndrome follows flexion injuries or disc fragments that damage the anterior spinal artery territory. The corticospinal and spinothalamic tracts lie anteriorly, so motor function, pain and temperature are lost while the dorsal columns keep vibration and position sense. The posterior spinal arteries feed the preserved dorsal columns. The artery of Adamkiewicz and the posterior inferior cerebellar artery serve other regions.
  35. The correct answer is D (Multiple myeloma with punched out lytic lesions of the skull): Multiple well defined lytic holes with no sclerotic rim, because there is little osteoblastic response, are the classic punched out lesions of myeloma. Anemia and bone pain in a man of his age support it, and serum protein electrophoresis is the next step. Paget disease thickens bone with a cotton wool pattern, sclerotic metastases are dense, and hyperparathyroidism gives a fine salt and pepper mottling.
  36. The correct answer is D (Hemochromatosis arthropathy, confirmed by raised transferrin saturation and serum ferritin): Hook-like osteophytes on the radial side of the second and third metacarpal heads with chondrocalcinosis suggest hemochromatosis arthropathy, and bronze skin with diabetes supports iron overload. Transferrin saturation and ferritin screen for it. Wilson disease causes a similar arthropathy but with liver and neurologic copper findings, and gout and isolated pyrophosphate disease do not explain the skin and diabetes.
  37. The correct answer is C (Moro reflex, which normally disappears by about 4 to 6 months of age): The Moro or startle reflex is elicited by sudden head extension and shows arm abduction and extension followed by adduction and crying. It normally fades by about 4 to 6 months, and persistence beyond that suggests a neurologic problem. The asymmetric tonic neck, Babinski and Galant reflexes are different responses elicited by other maneuvers.
  38. The correct answer is B (Vitamin C is a cofactor for hydroxylating proline and lysine, which collagen needs for stable cross-links): Scurvy results from vitamin C deficiency, which blocks hydroxylation of proline and lysine and produces weak collagen. Fragile vessels bleed around follicles and gums, and wounds heal poorly. Vitamin K deficiency affects clotting factors, copper deficiency affects lysyl oxidase, and vitamin B12 deficiency causes megaloblastic anemia.
  39. The correct answer is A (Hepatic gluconeogenesis from amino acids, lactate and glycerol sustains glucose while the brain oxidizes ketone bodies): By 48 hours liver glycogen is gone, so the liver makes glucose by gluconeogenesis from amino acids, lactate and glycerol. Fatty acid oxidation produces ketone bodies that the brain uses, sparing glucose and protein. Free fatty acids do not cross the blood-brain barrier well. Muscle lacks glucose 6-phosphatase and cannot export glucose, and the kidney does gluconeogenesis rather than glycolysis during fasting.
  40. The correct answer is B (Withhold manipulation and arrange urgent advanced imaging with communication to her oncologist): A cancer history, unexplained weight loss, night pain and pain unrelieved by rest are red flags for spinal metastasis, so the patient needs prompt imaging and coordinated oncology care. Manipulation over a possible lytic lesion risks pathologic fracture. A trial of conservative care delays diagnosis, and flexion and extension films are meant for instability, not for detecting marrow or bone destruction.
  41. The correct answer is D (Bacterial cystitis, most often from nitrate-reducing gram-negative organisms such as Escherichia coli): Positive nitrite means bacteria have converted urinary nitrate to nitrite, and positive leukocyte esterase shows pyuria, so together with bacteria and dysuria they indicate cystitis. Escherichia coli is the usual organism. A stone would more likely cause colicky flank pain with hematuria and little nitrite. Glomerulonephritis shows red cell casts and proteinuria rather than nitrite, and contamination would usually show squamous cells.
  42. The correct answer is A (Pathogen specific IgM in the newborn's serum, because IgM is too large to cross the placenta): IgG is the only antibody class that crosses the placenta, so a positive newborn IgG may simply mirror the mother's immunity. IgM is too large to cross, so pathogen specific IgM in the infant means the fetus made it in response to infection. Total IgG and pathogen IgG are therefore poor discriminators. Placental transfer of IgA is minimal, and IgA in the infant is not a standard test for congenital infection.
  43. The correct answer is C (Slowed median conduction across the wrist with a normal ulnar nerve localizes entrapment at carpal tunnel): Prolonged distal latencies of both motor and sensory median fibers with sparing of the ulnar nerve indicate a focal median neuropathy at the wrist, the electrodiagnostic hallmark of carpal tunnel syndrome. Root lesions are preganglionic, so sensory potentials stay normal. Pronator entrapment usually leaves distal wrist latencies normal, and a lower trunk lesion would produce ulnar abnormalities rather than sparing them.
  44. The correct answer is C (Celiac trunk, arising at T12 and supplying derivatives of the embryonic foregut): The celiac trunk is the first unpaired anterior branch of the abdominal aorta and arises at T12, supplying the foregut through the left gastric, splenic and common hepatic arteries. The superior mesenteric artery arises about one level lower at L1 and supplies the midgut. The inferior mesenteric artery arises at L3 for the hindgut. The renal arteries are paired lateral branches, so they do not feed the liver, stomach and spleen.
  45. The correct answer is A (A transient rise in pressure pain threshold near the treated segment with brief paraspinal EMG changes): Studies of spinal manipulation consistently report short-lived hypoalgesia, shown as higher pressure pain thresholds, and short-term changes in paraspinal EMG. Durable positional change, immune count changes, and sustained blood pressure effects are not established by good evidence.
  46. The correct answer is A (Normal anion gap metabolic acidosis with respiratory compensation from hyperventilation): The pH is low and bicarbonate is low, so this is metabolic acidosis, and PaCO2 is reduced because hyperventilation compensates. The anion gap of 138 minus 118 minus 14 equals 6, which is normal, and diarrheal bicarbonate loss is replaced by chloride retention. A high gap would occur with unmeasured acids. Respiratory acidosis would raise PaCO2, and alkalosis would raise pH.
  47. The correct answer is A (The sacral hiatus, bordered laterally by the two sacral cornua): The sacral hiatus results from failure of the laminae of S4 and S5 to fuse, leaving the canal open inferiorly. The sacral cornua are the remnants of the inferior articular processes and mark the hiatus on each side. The median sacral crest lies above this opening. The sacral promontory is an anterior landmark and the posterior foramina carry dorsal rami.
  48. Low-force positioning, pelvic blocking, a support belt and stabilization advice protect the joint without adding strain. High-velocity thrusts or end-range stretching of lax tissue risk further instability and pain.
  49. The correct answer is A (They convert x-ray energy to light more efficiently, so lower exposure gives the same film density): Rare earth screens such as gadolinium and lanthanum compounds convert a larger share of absorbed x-ray energy into light than calcium tungstate, so less radiation is needed to blacken the film. The trade is a slight loss of detail from quantum mottle. Added filtration, focal spot size and grids act on the beam or scatter, not on screen conversion efficiency.
  50. Pericarditis is usually positional and relieved by leaning forward, without local reproducible tenderness. Pulmonary embolism would more often bring tachycardia, hypoxia or dyspnea. Angina is exertional pressure that is not reproduced by touching the chest wall.
  51. Bragard dorsiflexion at the pain angle adds tension and confirms root irritation. Pain beyond 70 degrees is more often hamstring or joint related. The test is positive whenever symptoms are reproduced, not only at 90 degrees.
  52. The correct answer is D (Test for Helicobacter pylori and give combination therapy with a proton pump inhibitor if positive): Pain that eases with food and returns hours later, with nocturnal waking, is the classic duodenal ulcer pattern, and most such ulcers are associated with Helicobacter pylori. Eradication with a proton pump inhibitor plus antibiotics heals the ulcer and prevents recurrence. Antacids alone leave the infection untreated. Gastric cancer pain tends to worsen with eating, and biliary colic follows meals rather than easing with them.
  53. The correct answer is A (Langerhans cells, dendritic antigen-presenting cells derived from bone marrow precursors): Langerhans cells are bone marrow derived dendritic cells found in the stratum spinosum that take up antigen and carry it to regional nodes. Merkel cells are touch receptors in the basal layer, and melanocytes make pigment. Keratinocytes in the granular layer build the lipid barrier, so none of them present antigen.
  54. The correct answer is D (Tay-Sachs disease, with accumulation of GM2 ganglioside within neuronal lysosomes): The cherry-red macula, neurodegeneration with regression, hyperacusis, absent organomegaly and hexosaminidase A deficiency define Tay-Sachs disease. The missing enzyme leaves GM2 ganglioside to accumulate in neuronal lysosomes. Niemann-Pick also gives a cherry-red spot but has hepatosplenomegaly and a sphingomyelinase deficiency. Gaucher and Krabbe diseases involve different substrates and lack the macular finding.
  55. The correct answer is D (Butterfly vertebra, a developmental sagittal cleft that is a normal variant): Failure of the two lateral chondrification centers to fuse leaves a sagittal cleft, giving the butterfly appearance on the AP view. The adjacent bodies flare toward the cleft and there is no trauma history or sclerosis. A burst fracture shows cortical disruption and retropulsion, a hemangioma keeps a whole body with coarse vertical trabeculae, and a hemivertebra lacks half the body and produces angulation.
  56. The correct answer is C (The atlanto-occipital joint, between the occipital condyles and the superior facets of the atlas): The atlanto-occipital joints between the occipital condyles and the atlas allow flexion and extension, the nodding motion, with only a few degrees of side bending. The atlantoaxial articulations, including the median joint around the dens, provide about half of cervical rotation. Lower facet joints contribute to the larger cervical arcs but not primarily to nodding.
  57. The correct answer is C (Abnormal, below the normal 20 to 40 degrees, indicating a depressed posterior facet from calcaneal fracture): Boehler angle is drawn between a line from the highest point of the posterior facet to the highest point of the anterior process and a line from the posterior facet to the tuberosity, and normally falls between 20 and 40 degrees. A value of 10 degrees means the posterior facet has been compressed downward, as in an intra-articular calcaneal fracture. CT follows to define fragments, and the lumbar spine is also checked after a fall from height.
  58. The correct answer is C (Migraine with aura; reassure her, identify triggers, and co-manage with a physician if attacks are frequent): A visual aura that develops over minutes and is followed by a unilateral throbbing headache with nausea and photophobia, with a normal neurological examination, is typical of migraine with aura. The plan is reassurance, trigger identification and physician co-management when attacks are frequent. Cluster headaches are brief, orbital and accompanied by autonomic signs. A subarachnoid hemorrhage presents as a sudden thunderclap headache, not a gradual aura.
  59. The correct answer is B (A contact over the posterior rib angle near the costotransverse area, thrust timed to exhalation): Costovertebral tenderness and pain with breathing point to a restriction at the rib head or costotransverse joint, so the contact is over the rib angle or costotransverse area. Timing the thrust to exhalation lets the ribs settle and reduces guarding. Anterior contacts and spinous process thrusts do not address the posterior articulation that is painful.
  60. The correct answer is D (It stimulates ECL cells to release histamine and also acts on parietal cell CCK-B receptors): Gastrin from antral G cells acts mainly on ECL cells, releasing histamine that stimulates parietal cell H2 receptors, and it also binds parietal cell CCK-B receptors directly. Acetylcholine is the one acting through M3 receptors. Secretin raises bicarbonate and inhibits acid, and motility effects do not explain the high acid output.
  61. The correct answer is A (Hold sodium under 2,300 mg daily, ideally 1,500 mg, and emphasize fruits, vegetables, and low-fat dairy): The DASH pattern limits sodium to under 2,300 mg per day, with 1,500 mg as the ideal target for hypertensive adults, and emphasizes fruits, vegetables, whole grains and low-fat dairy. Potassium, calcium and magnesium from those foods help lower blood pressure. Higher sodium ceilings, high-fat protein substitutions and restricting potassium-rich produce all work against the pattern.
Case N-B09

Patient: F, 45 y, 65 in, 192 lb

Vitals: Pulse 96, resp 18, BP 128/78 mmHg, temp 100.4 F

Chief complaint and history: Elementary school teacher with sudden right upper abdominal pain that began 6 hours ago after a fried chicken dinner, now radiating to the right shoulder blade. Nausea with one episode of vomiting. Two shorter similar episodes in the past 3 months. Pain is constant. No chest pain or urinary symptoms. Takes an oral contraceptive.

Examination: No jaundice. Right upper quadrant tenderness with voluntary guarding. Murphy sign positive with inspiratory arrest. No rebound at McBurney point. Bowel sounds present. Spinal palpation from T6 to T9 nontender. Lungs clear.

Orthopedic and neurological tests: Cervical compression and shoulder depression negative. Right shoulder active and passive range full and painless. Empty can and Neer tests negative. Upper limb reflexes 2+ and strength 5/5. Thoracic rotation painless.

Laboratory and imaging: None ordered yet. Office findings: urine dipstick negative for blood and nitrite, pulse oximetry 98 percent. An ECG at urgent care yesterday for a similar episode was read as normal sinus rhythm.

  1. The correct answers are A and C: Visceral pain referred to the shoulder is not changed by shoulder or neck motion, and the shoulder examination here is normal. Meal related onset with nausea and recurrent attacks suggests biliary disease. Pain felt in the shoulder does not locate the lesion there. Absent appendiceal rebound and a normal ECG exclude only appendicitis and cardiac ischemia, not gallbladder disease.
  2. The correct answers are A and E: Right upper quadrant tenderness with inspiratory arrest on palpation, fever and fatty meal onset define acute cholecystitis, usually from a stone in the cystic duct. Earlier shorter episodes fit biliary colic from gallstones. Pancreatitis is epigastric and boring to the back, perforation produces diffuse rigidity, and pneumonia would not give a positive Murphy sign with clear lungs.
  3. The correct answers are C and E: Ultrasound is the first imaging study for suspected gallbladder disease and shows stones, a thickened wall and fluid around the gallbladder. The CBC shows leukocytosis, and the hepatic panel detects duct involvement through raised enzymes or bilirubin. Only about 10 to 15 percent of gallstones are visible on plain films. Neck imaging and cardiac stress testing do not address a picture that is clearly biliary.
  4. The correct answer is C (Hypermobility at L4-L5, with increased shear and rotational load on its disc and facets): When one segment loses motion, the total required range is taken up by neighbours, so the adjacent level becomes hypermobile and its disc and facets bear more shear and rotation. Stiffness does not simply spread upward, and the joints themselves do share the load. Bony fusion within months is not a recognized consequence of a mobile adjacent segment.
  5. The correct answer is D (The oximeter reads carboxyhemoglobin as if it were oxyhemoglobin because both absorb light at similar wavelengths): Standard two-wavelength pulse oximeters cannot distinguish carboxyhemoglobin from oxyhemoglobin, so the reading looks normal while oxygen delivery is poor. Carbon monoxide binds hemoglobin with about 200 times the affinity of oxygen and shifts the dissociation curve leftward, which impairs unloading. Plasma dissolved oxygen is a tiny fraction and is not what the device measures. Cyanide blocks cellular use of oxygen, but it does not create the false oximeter value seen here.
  6. The correct answer is D (Decompensated systolic heart failure with pulmonary and systemic venous congestion): Orthopnea, basilar crackles, an S3 gallop, elevated jugular venous pressure and pitting edema together show failure of both sides of the heart with volume overload. The S3 reflects rapid filling of a dilated ventricle. Pneumonia would not raise venous pressure or produce a gallop. Nephrotic edema usually has a normal jugular pressure, and cor pulmonale from lung disease shows wheeze and hyperinflation more than a gallop.
  7. The correct answer is B (Sac passes through the deep ring lateral to the inferior epigastric vessels): An indirect hernia follows the path of the spermatic cord through the deep inguinal ring, which lies lateral to the inferior epigastric vessels, and it can reach the scrotum. A direct hernia bulges through Hesselbach triangle medial to the vessels. Femoral and obturator hernias leave below the ligament or through the obturator canal.
  8. The correct answer is A (Slowed AV node conduction from high vagal tone, with every atrial impulse still reaching the ventricles): The PR interval measures conduction from atrial depolarization through the AV node to ventricular activation, and the AV node supplies most of that delay. Trained athletes have high vagal tone, which slows AV nodal conduction and produces benign first-degree block. Since every P wave still conducts, this is not second-degree block. Bundle branch delay would widen the QRS instead.
  9. The correct answer is B (Osteoarthritis, with DIP and first carpometacarpal involvement, sparing the MCP joints, with no erosions): Degenerative disease of the hands favors the distal interphalangeal joints and the first carpometacarpal joint, with narrowing, osteophytes, and subchondral sclerosis, and it spares the metacarpophalangeal joints. Rheumatoid arthritis targets the MCP and PIP joints with erosions and osteopenia. Psoriatic arthritis adds periostitis. Calcium pyrophosphate disease selects the second and third MCP joints with chondrocalcinosis.
  10. The correct answer is A (Fever with chills and burning on urination, plus right costovertebral angle percussion tenderness): Fever, chills, dysuria and costovertebral angle tenderness together point to pyelonephritis, an upper urinary tract infection that presents as flank or back pain and needs medical care. Pain that varies with posture, local muscle tenderness with limited side bending, and pain reproduced by resisted extension all support a musculoskeletal cause. The systemic features and the percussion tenderness are the findings that change triage.
  11. The correct answer is D (The internal vertebral venous plexus, a valveless epidural network that permits retrograde flow from pelvic veins): The internal vertebral venous (Batson) plexus lies in the epidural space and has no valves, so pressure changes in the abdomen and pelvis can reverse flow and carry tumor cells straight to the vertebrae and skull base without passing through the lungs. The thoracic duct and arterial routes would deposit tumor in the lungs or elsewhere first. The portal system drains the gut to the liver, not the pelvis to the spine.
  12. The correct answer is D (Structural scoliosis is likely, so obtain a standing posteroanterior radiograph to measure the Cobb angle): A postural curve disappears on forward bending, whereas a rib prominence that persists reflects vertebral rotation and a structural curve. A scoliometer reading of 7 degrees or more warrants a standing full-spine radiograph to measure the Cobb angle and guide monitoring or referral. Bracing decisions depend on the measured angle and skeletal maturity, and the equal leg lengths exclude a lift as the explanation.
  13. The correct answer is C (Preformed heat-stable enterotoxin ingested in food, stimulating vomiting without requiring bacterial growth inside the host): A very short incubation of one to six hours with vomiting points to a preformed toxin, and Staphylococcus aureus is classically found in salads, custards and mayonnaise dishes left warm. The enterotoxin is heat stable and acts as a superantigen, stimulating emesis through gut neural pathways. The other toxins require bacterial growth and colonization in the intestine, giving longer incubation periods and diarrhea, fever or blood.
  14. The correct answer is D (Reed-Sternberg cell, a large binucleate cell with prominent eosinophilic nucleoli giving an owl-eye appearance): Reed-Sternberg cells are neoplastic B cells with two mirror-image nuclei and large nucleoli, and they stain CD15 and CD30. They are needed for the diagnosis. Smudge cells belong to chronic lymphocytic leukemia, Auer rods to myeloid leukemia, and the starry-sky pattern with tingible body macrophages to Burkitt lymphoma.
  15. The correct answer is A (Middle meningeal artery, a branch of the maxillary artery running inside the skull): The pterion overlies the anterior branch of the middle meningeal artery, which runs in a groove on the inner skull. A fracture here tears the artery and produces an epidural hematoma, classically with a lucid interval before drowsiness. Bridging veins cause subdural bleeding and the scalp and cerebral arteries are not in the fracture line.
  16. The correct answer is D (Left conductive hearing loss, since sound lateralizes toward the ear with impaired air conduction): In conductive loss the affected ear hears bone-conducted sound louder because background noise is masked, so Weber lateralizes toward it. A negative Rinne, with bone conduction better than air conduction, confirms the conductive defect on the left. In sensorineural loss Weber lateralizes toward the normal ear and Rinne stays positive. Normal hearing would give a midline Weber.
  17. The correct answer is A (Isthmic spondylolisthesis, from a pars interarticularis defect with forward slip of L5 on the sacrum): Repeated hyperextension in adolescent gymnasts stresses the pars interarticularis and can cause a stress fracture that allows L5 to slip forward. Pain with extension, a palpable step-off and tight hamstrings fit this. Degenerative slippage occurs mainly at L4 in older women. Scheuermann disease is a thoracic wedging disorder, and a disc herniation would hurt more with sitting and flexion than with arching.
  18. The correct answer is B (5'-AUG-3', the start codon that specifies methionine at the beginning of protein synthesis): RNA is synthesized antiparallel and complementary to the template, with uracil pairing to adenine. Pairing 3'-TAC-5' gives 5'-AUG-3'. That is the start codon for methionine, and it matches the coding strand sequence 5'-ATG-3' with uracil replacing thymine. Copying the template, forgetting antiparallel orientation, or leaving thymine in the RNA each produce a wrong codon.
  19. The correct answer is A (Virchow node, signaling probable metastasis from an abdominal primary such as gastric carcinoma): A hard, fixed left supraclavicular node is Virchow node, reached by the thoracic duct draining abdominal lymph, and it suggests abdominal malignancy, especially gastric. Early satiety and weight loss fit gastric carcinoma. The Delphian node lies in front of the larynx, and Sister Mary Joseph node is at the umbilicus. A reactive node would be small, soft, tender and mobile.
  20. The correct answer is C (Segmental contact, naming the vertebral landmark that you are touching): The six graded elements are patient position, doctor position, segmental contact, doctor contact, stabilization and line of drive. The candidate named five of them and never said which vertebral landmark is being contacted, so segmental contact is missing. Preload, the setup name and thrust depth are part of good habit or the demonstration, but they are not among the six graded elements.
  21. The correct answer is B (Exertional heat stroke, with core temperature above 40 C and central nervous system dysfunction): Core temperature above 40 C together with altered mental status defines heat stroke, and exertional heat stroke can occur with moist skin. Heat exhaustion has a lower core temperature and normal cognition. Hyponatremic encephalopathy is possible in endurance events but would not produce a temperature this high. Heat cramps involve only muscle spasm. Heat stroke requires immediate whole-body cooling.
  22. The correct answer is A (A calcified structure within the abdominal cavity such as a splenic artery or a gallstone-sized density): Comparing both views localizes the density.
