Gout produces punched out marginal erosions with overhanging edges and tophi, not linear chondrocalcinosis. Hydroxyapatite deposition occurs in tendons and bursae rather than in cartilage.
  34. The correct answer is B (Functional residual capacity of 2.3 L, equal to expiratory reserve volume plus residual volume): After a normal quiet exhalation the lungs still hold the expiratory reserve volume plus the residual volume, which is 1.1 plus 1.2, equal to 2.3 L. That sum is the functional residual capacity. Inspiratory capacity, vital capacity and total lung capacity all include volumes that are exhaled or inhaled beyond the resting end expiratory point.
  35. The correct answer is A (The suboccipital nerve, the dorsal ramus of C1 emerging above the posterior arch of the atlas): The suboccipital muscles are supplied by the suboccipital nerve, which is the dorsal ramus of C1 and passes between the occiput and the atlas, above the posterior arch. The greater occipital nerve, from C2, is mostly cutaneous and passes below the inferior oblique. The lesser occipital nerve is sensory, and the accessory nerve supplies trapezius and sternocleidomastoid.
  36. The correct answer is B (Cytosine, thymine and uracil, which each contain a single six-membered nitrogenous ring called a pyrimidine): Pyrimidines are cytosine, thymine and uracil, and each has a single six-membered ring. Purines are adenine and guanine, which have a fused double-ring structure. A simple memory aid is that cut, thy and uracil are pyrimidines, and pure as gold identifies the purines. The distractors either place a purine in the pyrimidine group or describe purines instead of pyrimidines.
  37. The correct answer is D (S1 nerve root, which supplies the Achilles reflex and plantar flexion): The Achilles reflex and plantar flexion through gastrocnemius and soleus are S1 functions, and the posterior thigh to lateral foot pain follows the S1 dermatome. L5 compression would weaken great toe extension and spare the ankle jerk. L4 affects the patellar reflex and tibialis anterior, while L3 involvement would give anterior thigh pain and a weak quadriceps.
  38. The correct answer is D (Neutrophilia with a left shift consistent with acute bacterial infection): Mild to moderate leukocytosis with neutrophil predominance and increased band forms is a left shift, the marrow's response to acute bacterial infection. Viral illness usually produces lymphocytosis or a normal to low white count. Eosinophils are not increased in this differential. Chronic myeloid leukemia shows counts often above 50,000 with basophilia and immature granulocytes at several stages.
  39. The correct answer is A (Defer high-velocity thrusts, use gentle methods if any, and call the prescribing physician): An INR of 3.8 is above his therapeutic target, so the risk of hemorrhage from forceful techniques such as spinal hematoma or deep tissue bleeding is increased. High-velocity thrusts are deferred in favor of gentle methods, and the prescriber who manages the warfarin is contacted. Changing an anticoagulant dose is outside the chiropractor's scope, and waiting for bleeding to appear defeats prevention.
  40. The correct answer is A (The tunnel view, also called the Camp-Coventry view, with the knee flexed): The tunnel view, taken with the knee flexed and the beam angled, projects the intercondylar notch and the posterior femoral condyles in profile, which is where osteochondritis dissecans and loose bodies are found. The sunrise view shows the patellofemoral joint, the standing AP view shows joint space width and alignment, and a cross table lateral is best for a lipohemarthrosis, so none of these profile the notch. Standard AP and lateral films can miss these lesions because the notch lies behind the condyles.
  41. The correct answer is B (Axillary nerve, losing deltoid abduction and sensation over the lateral shoulder): The axillary nerve wraps around the surgical neck with the posterior circumflex humeral artery and supplies deltoid and teres minor. Injury flattens the deltoid, weakens abduction beyond the first degrees and numbs the lateral shoulder through the superior lateral brachial cutaneous branch. Radial injury causes wrist drop, musculocutaneous injury weakens elbow flexion, and suprascapular injury leaves the skin intact.
  42. The correct answer is C (Human chorionic gonadotropin from the trophoblast, acting on LH receptors to sustain progesterone secretion): The trophoblast of the implanted embryo secretes hCG, which binds the LH receptor on luteal cells and rescues the corpus luteum from regression. The preserved corpus luteum continues to secrete progesterone until the placenta takes over around weeks 8 to 10. Pituitary LH and FSH are suppressed by rising steroid levels. Estradiol does not rescue the corpus luteum, and hCG is also the analyte in pregnancy tests.
  43. The correct answer is C (The S1 root, typically compressed by an L5-S1 posterolateral disc herniation): The Achilles reflex and plantar flexion are supplied mainly by S1, and pain to the lateral foot fits the S1 dermatome. A posterolateral L5-S1 herniation typically compresses the S1 root. L5 compression weakens great toe extension and heel walking without losing the ankle reflex. L4 affects the patellar reflex and medial leg, and S2 does not explain the combined findings.
  44. The correct answer is D (Systemic lupus erythematosus, with Jaccoud arthropathy from capsular and ligamentous laxity): Reducible ulnar deviation and metacarpophalangeal subluxation without bone erosion in a woman with rash and fever is Jaccoud arthropathy, a soft tissue deformity of lupus. Rheumatoid arthritis produces fixed deformity with erosions and periarticular osteopenia. Scleroderma gives tuft resorption and calcinosis, and psoriatic arthritis involves the distal joints with periostitis.

Practice Test 7 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is C (Defer manipulation and refer for thoracic radiographs, with bone density evaluation as the next step.): Long term corticosteroid use causes osteoporosis, and a minor strain such as a sneeze can produce a vertebral compression fracture with focal bony tenderness. Manipulation is contraindicated until the spine is imaged and the fracture is excluded or managed. Soft tissue care alone and lumbar MRI neither protect the patient nor evaluate the painful level.
  2. The correct answer is A (Sexual relations with a current patient are misconduct even with consent, given the power imbalance): Boards treat sexual contact with a current patient as misconduct because the doctor holds power and the patient is vulnerable, so consent is not a defense. Transfer within the same office, waived fees or board notification do not change the rule. The doctor must end the treatment relationship properly and, in many states, wait a set period before any personal relationship.
  3. The correct answer is D (Factors II, VII, IX and X lose gamma-carboxylation because warfarin blocks vitamin K recycling): Vitamin K is the cofactor for gamma-glutamyl carboxylase, which adds carboxyl groups to glutamate residues on factors II, VII, IX and X. These carboxylated residues bind calcium and let the factors attach to phospholipid surfaces. Warfarin inhibits vitamin K epoxide reductase, so reduced vitamin K cannot be regenerated and the factors are made in inactive form. Heparin, not warfarin, acts through antithrombin, and vitamin C is not involved in clotting factor carboxylation.
  4. The correct answer is B (44 mmHg, which is within normal limits since the typical value is near 40 mmHg): Pulse pressure is systolic minus diastolic, and 128 minus 84 equals 44 mmHg, which is close to the normal value of about 40 mmHg. Adding the numbers gives 212 and is the wrong operation. The figure of 99 is the mean arterial pressure, calculated as 84 plus one third of 44. Half of 44 is 22, which has no role in this calculation.
  5. The correct answer is B (In 1895 in Davenport, Iowa, D.D. Palmer gave the first adjustment to Harvey Lillard, a janitor): D.D. Palmer performed the first adjustment on Harvey Lillard in Davenport, Iowa, in 1895, and the school followed in 1897. B.J. Palmer was his son and a later developer of the college. Kirksville and the year 1874 belong to Andrew Taylor Still and osteopathy. Chicago in 1910 does not match the founding.
  6. The correct answer is A (The mandibular division of the trigeminal nerve, which also carries general sensation from the lower face): The muscles of mastication, namely the temporalis, masseter and the medial and lateral pterygoids, are supplied by the motor root that travels with the mandibular division of the trigeminal nerve. When the pterygoids are weak on one side the jaw deviates toward that side on opening. The facial nerve supplies the muscles of expression, which are normal. The maxillary division is purely sensory.
  7. The correct answer is C (Withhold cervical manipulation and obtain flexion and extension lateral radiographs to assess the atlantodental interval): Neck pain with new hand clumsiness in a child with Down syndrome raises concern for atlantoaxial instability with cord compromise. Manipulation, traction or any provocative maneuver could cause catastrophic injury, so care is withheld until flexion and extension lateral films measure the atlantodental interval. A negative Sharp-Purser test is not reliable enough to clear him, and low-force methods still load the upper cervical spine.
  8. The correct answer is D (Isthmic spondylolisthesis, with anterior slip of L5 on S1 from a pars interarticularis defect): Isthmic spondylolisthesis results from a pars defect, often a stress fracture in adolescents who do repeated extension, and it usually involves L5 on S1. The film shows a lucency through the pars with the L5 body shifted forward. Degenerative slips occur in older adults with an intact pars, and retrolisthesis is posterior. The dysplastic form has an intact pars with deficient facets.
  9. The correct answer is B (Treatment may proceed with modification or caution, such as gentler technique, after weighing the risk): A relative contraindication means the treatment can be used with modification or caution, depending on clinical judgment and the patient's informed choice. For this patient that could mean a lower-force technique or a different approach for the neck. Prohibition until resolution describes an absolute contraindication. Proceeding unchanged ignores the added risk, and per-visit written approval is not the rule.
  10. The correct answer is C (Collimate to the lumbar region and place a lead contact shield over the testes): Tight collimation limits the primary beam and scatter to the area of interest. A properly placed contact shield over the testes lowers gonadal dose without hiding the lumbar vertebrae. Slower film speed requires more exposure, and halving the mA while doubling the time leaves the mAs and dose unchanged. A grid improves contrast but increases patient dose.
  11. The correct answer is D (Reduced hypothalamic GnRH pulse frequency, causing low pituitary gonadotropins and low ovarian estrogen output): Low FSH, LH and estradiol with normal prolactin and TSH localize the defect to the hypothalamus, where energy deficiency and heavy training reduce GnRH pulsatility. Ovarian failure would raise FSH and LH from lost feedback, and a prolactinoma requires elevated prolactin. A uterine anomaly would leave the hormone profile normal.
  12. The correct answer is C (Absence seizure, a generalized nonconvulsive type with abrupt onset, short unresponsiveness and no postictal confusion): Childhood absence epilepsy produces frequent 5 to 15 second lapses of awareness with immediate recovery, often provoked by hyperventilation, and the classic EEG is generalized 3 Hz spike-and-wave. Focal seizures with impaired awareness begin with auras and show focal temporal discharges with postictal confusion. Tonic-clonic seizures involve convulsions and a long postictal state, while atonic seizures cause falls and show slow spike-and-wave of about 2 Hz or less.
  13. The correct answer is C (An IgM autoantibody directed against the Fc portion of the patient's own IgG): Rheumatoid factor is an IgM, and sometimes IgA or IgG, autoantibody that binds the Fc portion of IgG. It is present in most patients with rheumatoid arthritis but is not specific. Anti-CCP antibodies are a separate and more specific marker. Anti-dsDNA and anti-Smith antibodies point to lupus, and the Fab and IgA description does not describe any recognized rheumatoid marker.
  14. The correct answer is A (S1 radiculopathy, commonly from an L5-S1 posterolateral disc herniation): The Achilles reflex, plantar flexion and sensation of the lateral foot are all supplied by the S1 root, most often compressed by an L5-S1 posterolateral herniation. L5 compression weakens dorsiflexion and the great toe and spares the Achilles reflex. Piriformis entrapment would not be provoked by lumbar flexion. L4 involvement alters the patellar reflex and medial leg sensation.
  15. The correct answer is C (The exiting L4 root, with weak knee extension and a reduced patellar reflex): A far lateral herniation at L4 to L5 lies in or beyond the foramen and catches the exiting L4 root rather than the traversing L5 root. The L4 root supplies the quadriceps and the patellar reflex, and its dermatome covers the anterior thigh and medial leg. A typical posterolateral herniation at the same level would compress the L5 root. The Achilles reflex and plantar flexion belong to S1.
  16. The correct answer is B (Wells scoring counts calf swelling, pitting edema and recent surgery; Homans sign is unreliable.): The Wells criteria score clinical features such as calf swelling, pitting edema, recent surgery or immobilization, tenderness along the deep veins and active cancer. Homans sign, calf pain on dorsiflexion, is neither sensitive nor specific, so it cannot rule DVT in or out. A patient with these features needs urgent medical evaluation and imaging, not manipulation or massage of the calf.
  17. The correct answer is C (Third part, lying lateral to the lower border of pectoralis minor): The axillary artery is divided by pectoralis minor into three parts. The third part, lateral to the muscle, gives the subscapular artery and the anterior and posterior circumflex humeral arteries, and it ends at the lower border of teres major. The first part gives the superior thoracic artery. The second part gives the thoracoacromial and lateral thoracic arteries, and beyond teres major the vessel is the brachial artery, which gives the profunda brachii.
  18. The correct answer is A (Numbness in the saddle region with new difficulty starting urination and reduced bowel control): Saddle anesthesia with new bladder or bowel dysfunction signals cauda equina syndrome, which needs emergency decompression to prevent permanent deficit. A trial of care is no longer appropriate. Pain worse with sitting and flexion is a typical disc pattern. Unilateral L5 radiculopathy and brief morning stiffness do not indicate sacral root compression.
  19. The correct answer is B (The thyroid rises with the larynx on swallowing, letting the examiner feel its isthmus and lobes): The thyroid is attached to the larynx and trachea by pretracheal fascia, so it moves upward during swallowing and slides under the examiner's fingers, which makes the isthmus, lobes and any nodules easier to define. The gland rises rather than descends. Swallowing does not relax the neck muscles in a useful way for this technique and does not create a bruit.
  20. The correct answer is B (85 percent, calculated as true positives divided by all children who truly have the disease): Sensitivity is true positives divided by true positives plus false negatives: 170 of the 200 diseased children, or 170 / 200 = 0.85, which is 85 percent. Specificity would be 250 / 300 = 83 percent, positive predictive value 170 / 220 = 77 percent, and negative predictive value 250 / 280 = 89 percent. Each wrong value is a real statistic from the same table but answers a different question than the one asked.
  21. The correct answer is C (Acute cholecystitis from gallstone impaction of the cystic duct): Right upper quadrant pain after a fatty meal, fever, shoulder blade radiation and inspiratory arrest on palpation of the gallbladder fossa is the Murphy sign of acute cholecystitis. A gallstone blocking the cystic duct causes distension and inflammation. Pancreatitis causes central pain going to the back, appendicitis localizes to the right lower quadrant, and duodenal ulcer pain tends to improve with meals.
  22. The correct answer is A (Normal, since it falls within the expected range of about 26 to 57 degrees): The Ferguson angle is the angle between the sacral base and the horizontal, with a normal range of about 26 to 57 degrees and a mean near 41. A value of 48 degrees falls within that range, so it is not hyperlordosis or hypolordosis. Extra imaging adds exposure without changing management.
  23. The correct answer is B (Dorsal column medial lemniscus pathway for joint position sense): A positive Romberg test means balance depends on vision because proprioception is lost. Position sense travels in the dorsal columns and medial lemniscus, and diabetic neuropathy commonly damages it. Cerebellar ataxia is unsteady with eyes open as well. The spinothalamic tract carries pain and temperature, and vestibular disease usually comes with vertigo.
  24. The correct answer is D (The gland rises with swallowing and feels smooth, soft, and nontender without nodules): A normal thyroid moves upward with the larynx during swallowing, and a normal gland feels smooth, soft and nontender without nodules. Fixation to surrounding tissue or a tender firm gland suggests thyroiditis or invasive disease. Downward movement is not seen, and a bruit suggests increased vascularity as in hyperthyroidism. A firm granular feel is abnormal even though the gland moves.
  25. The correct answer is A (Just above the superior border of the lower rib, away from the costal groove): The intercostal vein, artery and nerve, in that order from top to bottom, run in the costal groove along the inferior border of each rib. A needle therefore passes just above the upper surface of the lower rib to stay clear of the bundle belonging to the rib above. Midspace entry risks collateral branches, and entry under the upper rib lies directly in the groove.
  26. The correct answer is C (Grade II, which covers a slip between 25 and 50 percent of the sacral endplate width): The Meyerding system divides the superior sacral endplate into quarters, so a 30 percent slip falls in grade II, which spans 25 to 50 percent. Grade I is up to 25 percent, grade III is 50 to 75 percent and grade IV is 75 to 100 percent. A slip beyond the full endplate width is called spondyloptosis. In a young athlete this slip usually follows a bilateral pars defect.
  27. The correct answer is C (Emergency MRI and surgical consultation without delay, since cauda equina decompression is time sensitive): Saddle anesthesia, urinary retention, bilateral leg weakness and reduced rectal tone indicate compression of the cauda equina. Outcome depends on decompressing within hours, so the patient needs immediate MRI and surgical consultation. Manipulation and delayed care risk permanent bladder, bowel and sexual dysfunction. Plain radiographs cannot visualize the compressing disc, and muscle relaxants do not address a structural emergency.
  28. The correct answer is B (Prescribe McKenzie extension exercises such as repeated prone press-ups several times daily): Pain that moves from the leg toward the spine and eases with a repeated movement shows centralization and a directional preference, here toward backward bending. Repeated prone press-ups follow that preference and are the best match. Flexion peripheralizes his symptoms, there is no lateral shift described, and generic stretching ignores the response that guides the program.
  29. The correct answer is B (Disseminated Neisseria gonorrhoeae infection, a bacteremia producing arthritis, tenosynovitis and dermatitis together): Migratory polyarthralgia, tenosynovitis and pustular skin lesions with fever form the classic triad of disseminated gonococcal infection, which arises from untreated mucosal disease that may be silent. Secondary syphilis gives a diffuse rash including palms and soles. Reactive arthritis is sterile and follows urethritis with conjunctivitis. Rheumatoid arthritis is chronic and symmetric.
  30. The correct answer is C (It is abnormal for an adult, so avoid cervical manipulation and refer for evaluation): The atlantodental interval has an adult upper limit of about 3 mm, and a value of 6 mm indicates transverse ligament laxity or rupture. Rheumatoid pannus often erodes this ligament, and atlantoaxial instability can threaten the cord. Cervical manipulation should be avoided and the patient referred for flexion-extension and advanced imaging. Tolerating the finding or applying traction risks serious injury.
  31. The correct answer is D (Excess growth hormone drives hepatic IGF-1 production, which mediates tissue growth, and also raises glucose): Growth hormone acts on the liver to make IGF-1, which mediates most growth effects, so both are elevated in acromegaly. Growth hormone also antagonizes insulin and raises blood glucose, and failure to suppress it during glucose loading confirms autonomous secretion. IGF-1 exerts negative feedback on the pituitary rather than driving growth hormone release, and ACTH excess causes Cushing syndrome.
  32. The correct answer is A (28 degrees, so brace the patient and repeat standing films every four to six months): The Cobb angle is the angle between the two endplate tangents, and because they tilt in opposite directions the angles add, giving 17 plus 11, or 28 degrees. A curve of 25 to 40 degrees in a skeletally immature adolescent with Risser grade 0 to 2 and growth remaining has a high risk of progression, so bracing with serial films every four to six months is indicated. Surgery is considered when curves exceed about 45 to 50 degrees, and observation alone is for curves under 25 degrees.
  33. The correct answer is A (Postsynaptic nicotinic acetylcholine receptors on the muscle membrane at the neuromuscular junction): Myasthenia gravis is caused by antibodies to postsynaptic acetylcholine receptors, which cause fatigable weakness. Edrophonium inhibits acetylcholinesterase, which prolongs acetylcholine action and briefly improves strength. Presynaptic calcium channel antibodies cause Lambert-Eaton syndrome, and myelin antibodies are linked to demyelinating neuropathy.
  34. The correct answer is A (The lower trunk of the brachial plexus as it arches over the cervical rib): A cervical rib can tent the lower trunk of the brachial plexus (C8 and T1), producing medial hand and forearm numbness and intrinsic hand wasting. A C8 radiculopathy gives a similar pattern but typically is reproduced by neck movement and Spurling test, which are negative here. Upper trunk compression affects the shoulder and lateral arm, and carpal tunnel spares the hypothenar region and medial forearm.
  35. The correct answer is A (Medial compartment, containing gracilis and the adductor muscles that pull the thigh inward): The obturator nerve leaves the pelvis through the obturator canal and supplies the medial compartment, which adducts the thigh. Loss causes weak adduction and a patch of medial thigh numbness. The anterior compartment belongs to the femoral nerve and the posterior compartment to the sciatic nerve, while the gluteal muscles are supplied by the superior and inferior gluteal nerves.
  36. The correct answer is A (Order foot radiographs, because tenderness at the fifth metatarsal base meets Ottawa foot criteria): The Ottawa rules call for a foot series when there is tenderness at the base of the fifth metatarsal or the navicular, or when the patient cannot bear weight for four steps. She meets both criteria, so a fracture such as an avulsion must be excluded before treatment. Malleolar tenderness alone triggers the ankle series, which does not apply here, and waiting a week is not justified when the rule is positive.
  37. The correct answer is D (C7 root, commonly compressed by a disc herniation at the C6-C7 level): The triceps reflex, wrist flexion and the middle finger belong to C7, and a C6-C7 disc usually compresses the exiting C7 root. C6 would reduce the biceps and brachioradialis reflexes with thumb numbness, and those reflexes are normal here. C8 would weaken finger flexors and spare the triceps reflex, with numbness along the little finger. A lower trunk lesion would produce a broader hand pattern rather than one reflex and one dermatome.
  38. The correct answer is A (Valine replaces glutamate at position 6, creating a hydrophobic patch that promotes deoxyhemoglobin polymerization): Sickle hemoglobin results from a single base change that substitutes valine for glutamate at position 6 of the beta chain. The nonpolar valine forms a hydrophobic patch that binds a complementary pocket on another deoxyhemoglobin molecule, producing polymers and sickled red cells. Lysine at position 6 describes hemoglobin C. Polymerization occurs mainly in the deoxygenated state, and the substitution runs from glutamate to valine.
  39. The correct answer is D (30 pack-years, which meets the 20 pack-year threshold used for annual low-dose CT screening): Pack-years equal packs per day multiplied by years smoked, so 1.5 x 20 = 30, and rechecking, 20 x 1 = 20 plus 20 x 0.5 = 10 gives 30. Screening criteria use a 20 pack-year history in adults aged 50 to 80 who smoke or quit within 15 years, so he qualifies. The other values come from skipping the multiplication or adding instead of multiplying, and 40 is not the threshold.
  40. The correct answer is C (Urgent referral for advanced imaging and oncologic evaluation, with no spinal adjustment to the region): A plan has to follow from the assessment: suspected metastasis in a patient with prior cancer, night pain and weight loss needs urgent advanced imaging and oncologic evaluation without loading the spine. Mobilization, even gentle, still stresses a vertebra that may be weakened. A six week trial of conservative care is for mechanical pain with no red flags. Adjusting first and imaging after three visits puts the risk of pathologic fracture ahead of diagnosis.
  41. The correct answer is C (Pleural effusion, with fluid separating the lung from the chest wall at the base): Dullness, absent breath sounds and decreased tactile fremitus over a dependent area indicate fluid between the pleural layers, and heart failure is a common cause. Pneumonia increases fremitus and gives bronchial breathing, and pneumothorax is hyperresonant. Atelectasis can be dull with reduced sounds, but it shifts the trachea toward the lesion and is less likely with the heart failure history.
  42. The correct answer is B (The fourth lumbar vertebra, roughly level with the highest points of both iliac crests): The abdominal aorta begins at T12 and ends by dividing into the common iliac arteries at L4, which is about the level of the supracristal plane through the iliac crests. The renal arteries arise much higher, near L1 to L2, and the aortic hiatus is the T12 entry point. The sacral level is where the iliac vessels have already descended into the pelvis, so none of those fit a bifurcation seen at the iliac crests.
  43. The correct answer is B (Chronic obstructive pulmonary disease with a chronic bronchitis phenotype): A post-bronchodilator FEV1/FVC below 0.70 confirms persistent airflow limitation, and in a long-term smoker with chronic productive cough this is chronic obstructive pulmonary disease. The lack of reversal argues against asthma, which typically shows variability and improvement of at least 12 percent and 200 mL. Pulmonary fibrosis would give a normal or high ratio with reduced volumes. Bronchiectasis is defined by imaging and large daily sputum volumes, which are not described.
  44. The correct answer is C (Airborne transmission by small aerosol particles that stay suspended and travel beyond the room): Measles virus travels on aerosol particles small enough to remain suspended for hours and drift through ventilation, so airborne precautions with a negative-pressure room are required. Droplet spread involves larger particles that settle quickly, which is why influenza needs only a mask and private room. A fomite is an inanimate object, and contact transmission needs touch, so neither accounts for persistence in room air.
  45. Segmentation is the mixing pattern after meals. The gastrocolic reflex is triggered by stretch of the stomach after eating and increases colonic motility, so it does not depend on the rectum or CCK.

A sequestered fragment has lost continuity with the parent disc. A bulge is broad-based and involves more than half of the circumference, not a focal displacement.

Observation applies to curves under about 25 degrees, and surgery is generally considered above 45 to 50 degrees.

Both results here point to the right ear. In sensorineural loss the Weber lateralizes to the better ear and air conduction stays louder than bone. A normal Rinne always has air conduction louder than bone, so this result is not normal.

The correct answer is C (The absolute refractory period, because sodium channel inactivation gates are closed and cannot reopen): During the absolute refractory period inactivation gates on voltage-gated sodium channels are closed and cannot reopen until the membrane repolarizes, so no stimulus can trigger another spike. This limits firing frequency and ensures one-way propagation. In the relative refractory period a stronger stimulus can fire the cell. Resting channels can open, and the upstroke produces spikes.

Because the disease is rare, a case-control study is efficient, since a cohort would need enormous numbers to accrue enough cases.
