  1. Tell the patient what you will do, answer questions and get consent.
  2. Place the patient supine and cradle the head with both hands.
  3. Palpate the right C5 articular pillar and place the index finger contact there.
  4. Rotate the head gently away from the contact side and side bend slightly toward it.
  5. Take out the slack without reaching the end of the range.
  6. State the line of drive and confirm the patient is relaxed.

Safety checks

  • Screen for the 5 Ds and 3 Ns, sudden severe headache and neck pain before any rotational thrust.
  • Do not take the neck to end range rotation.
  • Stop if the patient reports dizziness, double vision or new neurological symptoms.

Common errors

  • Contacting the spinous process or soft tissue instead of the articular pillar.
  • Rotating the head too far before the thrust.
  • Not naming the stabilizing hand.

Grading points

  • Patient position stated and correct.
  • Doctor position and stance stated.
  • Contact on the right C5 articular pillar with the correct finger surface.
  • Stabilization by the other hand and the table named.
  • Line of drive follows the facet plane with low amplitude.
  • Patient informed and consent confirmed.

T02 (Cervical): Set up a side posture cervical adjustment for a left C4 restriction.

Patient position: Side lying with the left side up, head supported on a thin pillow in line with the spine.

Doctor position and stance: Standing at the head of the table facing the patient, weight even, thighs against the table edge.

Segment or listing: C4 restricted on the left, listed as left rotation.

Contact point: Segmental contact on the left C4 articular pillar. Doctor contact on the lateral index finger over the proximal phalanx.

Line of drive: Lateral to medial with a slight superior component, lined up with the facet plane of C4.

Setup steps

  1. Explain the procedure and ask permission.
  2. Position the patient on the right side with the head supported level.
  3. Locate the left C4 articular pillar by palpation.
  4. Place the index contact and cup the head with the other hand for stabilization.
  5. Rotate and side bend slightly to take out the slack.
  6. Say the line of drive aloud before the thrust.

Safety checks

  • Ask about neck pain with dizziness, vision change, numbness or recent trauma first.
  • Use light force and a short lever.
  • Defer if the patient cannot relax or the pain increases.

Common errors

  • Head tilted up or down so the neck is not level.
  • Contact placed on the transverse process instead of the pillar.
  • Line of drive across the facet plane rather than along it.

Grading points

  • Side lying position correct.
  • Doctor stands at the head facing the patient.
  • Articular pillar named as the segmental contact.
  • Doctor contact surface named.
  • Stabilization at the head.
  • Line of drive stated with the facet plane.

T03 (Cervical): Demonstrate the setup for an atlas adjustment with a specific contact.

Patient position: Side lying with the right (listed) side up, head supported level on a thin pillow, or supine for a hand held contact.

Doctor position and stance: At the head of the table, leaning slightly toward the patient with a stable stance.

Segment or listing: Atlas (C1) restricted on the right.

Contact point: Segmental contact on the right transverse process of the atlas, found below the mastoid and behind the ramus of the mandible. Doctor contact on the pad of the index finger.

Line of drive: Very low force impulse directed along the plane of the atlas joint, typically medial and slightly superior.

Setup steps

  1. Tell the patient the force is gentle and obtain consent.
  2. Position the head neutral with no rotation.
  3. Palpate the atlas transverse process behind the ramus.
  4. Place the index finger contact lightly and settle the hand.
  5. Stabilize the head with the other hand and the table.
  6. State the line of drive and deliver a small impulse in the demonstration.

Safety checks

  • Screen for cervical arterial signs and trauma before any upper neck work.
  • Never use end range rotation on the atlas.
  • Stop and refer for sudden severe headache, double vision or loss of balance.

Common errors

  • Contacting the mastoid or mandible instead of the transverse process.
  • Using too much force.
  • Wrong head position that adds rotation.

Grading points

  • Neutral head position.
  • Atlas transverse process named correctly.
  • Doctor contact surface named.
  • Stabilization named.
  • Line of drive and light force stated.
  • Safety screen mentioned.

T04 (Cervical): Set up an axis (C2) adjustment using the spinous process.

Patient position: Prone with the face in a headpiece, neck in neutral, or seated if the table allows.

Doctor position and stance: Standing at the side of the table near the head, facing the patient's neck.

Segment or listing: C2 with a spinous process rotated to the left.

Contact point: Segmental contact on the side of the C2 bifid spinous process or lamina. Doctor contact on the thumb or the lateral index finger.

Line of drive: Lateral to medial against the side of the spinous process, in the direction that reverses the rotation, with a very small amplitude.

Setup steps

  1. Explain and gain consent.
  2. Place the patient prone with the neck neutral.
  3. Locate the large C2 spinous process, the first one felt below the skull.
  4. Place the contact on the side of the spinous process.
  5. Stabilize with the other hand on the opposite side of the neck or the head.
  6. State the line of drive and the listing.

Safety checks

  • Screen for cervical arterial dysfunction and instability signs.
  • Keep force low.
  • Stop if numbness, dizziness or radiating pain starts.

Common errors

  • Contacting C3 or the occiput by mistake.
  • Pushing into the soft tissue instead of bone.
  • Thrust that adds flexion or extension.

Grading points

  • Correct patient position.
  • Doctor position stated.
  • C2 spinous process identified correctly.
  • Doctor contact named.
  • Stabilization hand named.
  • Line of drive fits the listing.

T05 (Cervical): Set up a seated or supine lower cervical adjustment with a lateral flexion restriction at C6.

Patient position: Supine with the head in the doctor's hands and shoulders relaxed.

Doctor position and stance: At the head of the table, facing the patient, elbows close to the body.

Segment or listing: C6 restricted in left lateral flexion.

Contact point: Segmental contact on the left C6 articular pillar. Doctor contact on the lateral proximal phalanx of the index finger.

Line of drive: Short thrust in the direction of lateral flexion along the plane of the facet, with a small rotary part and no extension.

Setup steps

  1. Tell the patient the plan and get consent.
  2. Set the head neutral on the table.
  3. Palpate the left C6 articular pillar.
  4. Place the contact and side bend the head toward the contact to take the slack out.
  5. Add a small amount of rotation away from the contact.
  6. State the line of drive aloud.

Safety checks

  • Screen for arterial dysfunction signs first.
  • Avoid end range positions.
  • Do not thrust if the patient is guarding or in severe pain.

Common errors

  • Too much rotation before the thrust.
  • Contact on the transverse process in front of the pillar.
  • Quick movement of the whole head instead of a focused thrust.

Grading points

  • Position correct.
  • Segment and listing stated.
  • Contact named.
  • Stabilization named.
  • Line of drive follows the facet.
  • Patient informed.

T06 (Thoracic): Set up a prone thoracic thrust with a cross bilateral transverse process contact at T6.

Patient position: Prone with the chest flat and arms relaxed at the sides, face in the headpiece.

Doctor position and stance: Standing at the side of the table facing the patient, feet apart with the shoulders over the contact.

Segment or listing: T6 with a restriction in right rotation.

Contact point: Segmental contact on the right transverse process of T6 and the left transverse process of T7, crossing the hands. Doctor contact on the pisiform of each hand.

Line of drive: Posterior to anterior with a slight cephalad component for the upper thoracic area, delivered at the end of exhalation.

Setup steps

  1. Explain and get consent.
  2. Position the patient prone and ask them to relax the shoulders.
  3. Palpate and find the T6 and T7 transverse processes.
  4. Place the crossed pisiform contacts on the transverse processes.
  5. Take out the slack by leaning your weight over the contact.
  6. Ask the patient to breathe out and state the line of drive.

Safety checks

  • Do not thrust in patients with osteoporosis, rib fracture or severe pain.
  • Use the lowest force that works.
  • Check for chest pain or breathing difficulty after the thrust.

Common errors

  • Contacts on the ribs instead of the transverse processes.
  • Thrust at the top of inhalation.
  • Arms bent so the force is lost.

Grading points

  • Position and stance correct.
  • Both transverse process contacts named.
  • Pisiform contacts stated.
  • Stabilization by the table and the opposite hand stated.
  • Line of drive and breathing phase stated.
  • Patient informed.

T07 (Thoracic): Set up a prone thoracic thrust using a single spinous process contact at T9.

Patient position: Prone with the chest flat and the head in the headpiece.

Doctor position and stance: Standing at the side of the table, facing the patient, with the shoulder directly above the contact hand.

Segment or listing: T9 posterior, listed as a posterior spinous process.

Contact point: Segmental contact on the T9 spinous process. Doctor contact on the pisiform of one hand, reinforced by the other hand.

Line of drive: Posterior to anterior with a neutral to slightly cephalad angle following the slope of the T9 spinous process, delivered at the end of exhalation.

Setup steps

  1. Tell the patient what you will do and ask permission.
  2. Position the patient prone and relaxed.
  3. Palpate the T9 spinous process.
  4. Place the pisiform on the spinous process and set the reinforcing hand over it.
  5. Straighten the elbows and lean over the contact to take out the slack.
  6. Ask for exhalation and state the line of drive.

Safety checks

  • Check bone health and any history of fracture.
  • Avoid thrusting over a spinal injury or a painful mass.
  • Stop if the pain increases.

Common errors

  • Contact over the transverse process or the rib.
  • Elbows bent.
  • A caudal angle that drives the segment into extension, or a thrust that ignores the slope of the spinous process.

Grading points

  • Prone position correct.
  • Spinous process contact named.
  • Pisiform contact stated.
  • Stabilization stated.
  • Line of drive stated with breathing.
  • Doctor stance correct.

T08 (Thoracic): Set up a supine cross arm thoracic adjustment at T5.

Patient position: Supine with the arms crossed over the chest, each hand on the opposite shoulder.

Doctor position and stance: Standing at the side of the table, facing the patient, with the chest or abdomen over the patient's crossed arms.

Segment or listing: T5 restricted in extension.

Contact point: Segmental contact on the T5 spinous process and transverse processes from beneath. Doctor contact with a closed fist or pocket contact placed under the patient's back.

Line of drive: Anterior to posterior through the segment with a slight inferior to superior component.

Setup steps

  1. Explain the procedure and ask about bone health.
  2. Have the patient cross the arms and roll toward you.
  3. Place the fist contact under T5 and roll the patient back onto it.
  4. Lean over the crossed arms and take out the slack.
  5. Ask the patient to breathe out.
  6. State the line of drive and give a controlled thrust in the demonstration.

Safety checks

  • Avoid this setup in osteoporosis, rib fracture or severe pain.
  • Check there is no breast or chest tenderness that makes the position unsafe.
  • Stop if the patient has chest pain or trouble breathing.

Common errors

  • Fist placed too high or too low on the spine.
  • Pressure on the patient's arms instead of the spine.
  • Not asking about bone density.

Grading points

  • Arm position correct.
  • Doctor contact named.
  • Segment identified.
  • Stabilization through the table and body stated.
  • Line of drive stated.
  • Bone health screened and patient informed.

T09 (Thoracic): Set up a posterior rib adjustment for a depressed left rib 6.

Patient position: Prone with the chest flat and the arms at the sides.

Doctor position and stance: Standing at the side of the table on the side of the rib, facing the patient.

Segment or listing: Left rib 6 depressed, restricted in inhalation.

Contact point: Segmental contact at the rib angle of the left sixth rib near the costotransverse joint. Doctor contact on the pisiform or thenar.

Line of drive: Along the curve of the rib, anterior and superior for a depressed rib, delivered at the phase of breathing that matches the restriction.

Setup steps

  1. Explain the plan and ask for consent.
  2. Position the patient prone and relaxed.
  3. Find the rib angle of rib 6 and feel it move with breathing.
  4. Place the pisiform on the rib angle and stabilize with the table and the other hand.
  5. Take out the slack along the rib curve.
  6. State the line of drive and the breathing phase.

Safety checks

  • Rule out rib fracture, pleurisy and osteoporosis first.
  • Use a broad soft contact.
  • Stop if the patient has sharp chest pain or shortness of breath.

Common errors

  • Contact on the transverse process rather than the rib angle.
  • Wrong line of drive for a depressed rib.
  • Thrusting during the wrong phase of breathing.

Grading points

  • Rib listing stated.
  • Rib angle contact named.
  • Doctor contact surface named.
  • Stabilization stated.
  • Line of drive follows the rib curve.
  • Safety screen mentioned.

T10 (Thoracic): Set up a seated first rib adjustment on the right.

Patient position: Seated on a stool or the table with the back straight and arms relaxed.

Doctor position and stance: Standing behind the patient with a stable stance, one foot slightly forward.

Segment or listing: Right first rib elevated.

Contact point: Segmental contact on the superior surface of the first rib, between the neck and the shoulder. Doctor contact on the web between the thumb and index finger.

Line of drive: Caudal and anterior toward the opposite hip.

Setup steps

  1. Tell the patient what you will do and ask permission.
  2. Seat the patient upright and relaxed.
  3. Palpate the superior surface of the first rib beside the trapezius.
  4. Place the web contact and cup the patient's head and shoulder with the other hand.
  5. Take out the slack with a small side bend of the head toward the contact.
  6. State the line of drive and deliver a light thrust in the demonstration.

Safety checks

  • Screen for thoracic outlet signs, nerve symptoms and trauma first.
  • Use light force over this sensitive area.
  • Stop if the patient has arm numbness or tingling.

Common errors

  • Contact on the clavicle or trapezius muscle only.
  • Line of drive directed upward.
  • Too much force.

Grading points

  • Seated position correct.
  • Doctor stands behind.
  • First rib superior surface contact named.
  • Doctor contact surface named.
  • Stabilization stated.
  • Line of drive caudal and anterior toward the opposite hip.

T11 (Lumbar): Set up a side posture lumbar roll for a right L4 restriction.

Patient position: Side lying with the right side up. Lower leg straight, upper leg flexed at the hip and knee with the foot hooked behind the lower knee. Lower arm forward, upper arm drawn back to rotate the trunk.

Doctor position and stance: Standing in front of the patient facing the table at about 45 degrees, with the thigh or knee against the patient's flexed upper knee.

Segment or listing: L4 restricted on the right.

Contact point: Segmental contact on the right mammillary process of L4. Doctor contact on the pisiform or the web of the hand.

Line of drive: Posterior to anterior and slightly inferior to superior along the facet plane with the rotation.

Setup steps

  1. Explain the procedure and get consent.
  2. Position the patient in side posture as described.
  3. Palpate the right L4 mammillary process.
  4. Place the contact and the stabilizing hand and forearm at the shoulder or upper arm.
  5. Rotate the trunk to take out the slack at L4 only.
  6. State the line of drive and deliver a gentle demonstration thrust.

Safety checks

  • Screen for cauda equina signs, fracture, severe radiculopathy and instability first.
  • Do not use if rotation produces leg pain or numbness.
  • Stop if the patient feels a sharp pain.

Common errors

  • Rotating the trunk too far so the force goes to the wrong level.
  • Contact on the spinous process when the listing calls for the mammillary process.
  • Doctor stance too wide or square to the table.

Grading points

  • Patient leg and arm position correct.
  • Doctor stance stated.
  • Mammillary process named.
  • Doctor contact surface named.
  • Stabilization points named.
  • Line of drive stated.

T12 (Lumbar): Set up a lumbar spinous push in side posture for a rotated L3 spinous process.

Patient position: Side lying, lower leg straight, upper leg flexed with the foot hooked behind the lower knee.

Doctor position and stance: Standing facing the patient at the table, feet apart, body over the contact.

Segment or listing: L3 with the spinous process rotated to the left.

Contact point: Segmental contact on the side of the L3 spinous process. Doctor contact on the pisiform or the thumb.

Line of drive: Against the side of the spinous process in the direction opposite to the rotation, to derotate the segment.

Setup steps

  2. Position the patient on the correct side for the listing.
  3. Locate the L3 spinous process and its rotation.
  4. Place the contact on the side of the spinous process.
  5. Stabilize with the other hand at the shoulder or knee.
  6. State the line of drive that reverses the rotation.

Safety checks

  • Screen for red flags and nerve signs before the thrust.
  • Use low force.
  • Stop for new pain, numbness or weakness.

Common errors

  • Pushing in the direction of the rotation instead of against it.
  • Contact on the interspinous space.
  • Not stating the listing first.

Grading points

  • Position correct.
  • Listing stated.
  • Spinous process contact named.
  • Doctor contact named.
  • Stabilization named.
  • Line of drive reverses the rotation.

T13 (Lumbar): Set up prone flexion distraction for the lower lumbar spine in a patient with disc involvement.

Patient position: Prone on a flexion distraction table with the pelvis at the break of the table, ankles secured or held lightly.

Doctor position and stance: Standing at the side of the table near the patient's hips, facing the table.

Segment or listing: L4 L5 disc involvement without cauda equina signs.

Contact point: Segmental contact on the L4 spinous process area. Doctor contact on the thenar or pisiform of one hand, cephalad direction.

Line of drive: Cephalad and anterior on the contacted segment while the table moves into flexion with cyclic distraction.

Setup steps

  1. Explain the procedure and tell the patient it is a gentle, non thrust method.
  2. Position the patient prone with the pelvis at the break.
  3. Locate L4 and place the contact on the spinous process.
  4. Hold the caudal section of the table with the other hand.
  5. Lower the caudal piece into flexion in a slow, cyclic motion.
  6. State the line of drive and check the patient's comfort.

Safety checks

  • Do not use with cauda equina signs, fracture, tumor or infection.
  • Stop if leg pain increases or spreads.
  • Keep the motion within the patient's comfort.

Common errors

  • Contact too high or too low.
  • Fast jerky table motion.
  • Forgetting to describe the table movement.

Grading points

  • Pelvis at the break stated.
  • Contact named.
  • Doctor contact surface named.
  • Table hand and motion named.
  • Line of drive cephalad and anterior stated.
  • Safety and comfort checks stated.

T14 (Lumbar): Set up a drop table adjustment for the lumbar spine at L5.

Patient position: Prone on a drop table with the pelvic piece set to the patient's size and the drop mechanism tensioned correctly.

Doctor position and stance: Standing at the side of the table with the shoulders over the contact and the table drop piece ready.

Segment or listing: L5 posterior.

Contact point: Segmental contact on the L5 spinous process or the mammillary process. Doctor contact on the pisiform with the other hand reinforcing.

Line of drive: Posterior to anterior, with a quick light thrust at the moment the table piece drops.

Setup steps

  1. Explain the table drop and the light thrust and get consent.
  2. Check and set the tension of the drop piece for the patient's weight.
  3. Position the patient prone with the pelvis on the piece.
  4. Palpate L5 and place the pisiform contact.
  5. Lean over the contact and take out the slack.
  6. State the line of drive and the timing of the drop.

Safety checks

  • Do not use in patients with fracture, severe osteoporosis, cauda equina signs or recent surgery.
  • Set tension to the patient's size, not too heavy.
  • Stop if the patient has leg pain or numbness.

Common errors

  • Drop tension wrong for the patient.
  • Thrust too hard in addition to the drop.
  • Contact off the segment.

Grading points

  • Table set up and tension stated.
  • Patient position correct.
  • Contact named.
  • Doctor contact named.
  • Stabilization by the table named.
  • Line of drive and timing stated.

T15 (Lumbar): Set up a lumbar mammillary process contact in a side posture for a left L5 restriction using a different arm position.

Patient position: Side lying with the left side up, lower leg straight, upper leg flexed with the foot behind the knee, lower arm across the chest, upper arm forward.

Doctor position and stance: Standing in front facing the table at about 45 degrees, the thigh against the patient's upper knee.

Segment or listing: L5 restricted on the left.

Contact point: Segmental contact on the left L5 mammillary process. Doctor contact on the index finger web or pisiform.

  1. Tell the patient and gain consent.
  2. Position the legs and arms as listed.
  3. Locate the left L5 mammillary process.
  4. Place the contact and the other forearm at the patient's shoulder.
  5. Rotate gently until the slack is out at L5.
  6. State the line of drive.

Safety checks

  • Check for leg pain with rotation, which means stop.
  • Do not use after recent surgery or with suspected fracture.
  • Use only a short, light thrust.

Common errors

  • Excessive rotation of the upper body.

T16 (Pelvic): Set up a side posture adjustment for a right posterior ilium.

T25 (Soft tissue): Set up a prone flexion distraction soft tissue and mobilization approach for low back muscle spasm.

Patient position: Prone on a flexion distraction table with the pelvis at the break.

Doctor position and stance: Standing at the side of the table at the patient's hip level, facing the table.

Segment or listing: Lumbar paraspinal spasm without nerve root signs.

Contact point: Contact over the lumbar paraspinal muscles with the thenar of the hand, with the other hand on the table piece.

Line of drive: Cephalad pressure into the muscles while the table moves slowly into gentle flexion.

Setup steps

  1. Tell the patient the plan and ask permission.
  2. Position the patient prone with the pelvis at the break.
  3. Locate the muscle spasm by palpation.
  4. Place the thenar contact beside the spinous processes.
  5. Hold the table piece and start a slow flexion motion.
  6. State the direction and keep checking comfort.

Safety checks

  • Rule out fracture, cauda equina signs and severe radiculopathy first.
  • Stop if leg pain increases.
  • Keep motion gentle.

Common errors

  • Pressing on the spinous processes instead of the muscle.
  • Fast table movement.
  • No comfort check.

Grading points

  • Table position correct.
  • Contact named.
  • Doctor contact named.
  • Table hand stated.
  • Direction of pressure and motion stated.
  • Patient comfort and consent addressed.

T26 (Soft tissue): Demonstrate a seated upper trapezius soft tissue release.

Patient position: Seated upright on a stool with the arms resting in the lap.

Doctor position and stance: Standing behind the patient with a stable stance.

Segment or listing: Right upper trapezius tension with a tender trigger point.

Contact point: Contact on the tender band of the upper trapezius. Doctor contact on the thumb pad or the flat of the fingers.

Line of drive: Steady sustained pressure into the muscle along the fiber direction, without twisting the neck.

Setup steps

  1. Explain the technique and ask permission.
  2. Seat the patient and relax the shoulders.
  3. Locate the tender band by palpation.
  4. Place the thumb pad on the trigger point.
  5. Stabilize the head with the other hand.
  6. Apply steady pressure, state the direction and ask about pain.

Safety checks

  • Rule out fracture, infection, blood clotting problems and skin conditions.
  • Use pressure the patient can tolerate.
  • Stop if the patient reports numbness, tingling or sharp pain.

Common errors

  • Too much pressure at first.
  • Pressing on the neck structures.
  • Not stabilizing the head.

Grading points

  • Seated position correct.
  • Doctor position stated.
  • Muscle and contact named.
  • Stabilization stated.
  • Direction of pressure stated.
  • Consent and pain feedback stated.

T27 (Soft tissue): Set up a supine piriformis stretch with muscle contraction (post isometric relaxation).

Patient position: Supine with the hip and knee flexed on the affected side.

Doctor position and stance: Standing at the side of the table, facing the patient, near the affected hip.

Segment or listing: Right piriformis tightness with deep buttock pain.

Contact point: Contact at the patient's knee and ankle, with the other hand stabilizing the opposite hip. Doctor contact on the knee and ankle.

Line of drive: Hip is taken into flexion and adduction toward the opposite shoulder, after a gentle contraction of the muscle that is then released.

Setup steps

  1. Explain the stretch and the gentle push the patient will do.
  2. Position the patient supine and flex the hip and knee.
  3. Stabilize the opposite hip with one hand.
  4. Take the leg to the first point of stretch.
  5. Ask the patient to push gently against your hand for about 5 to 10 seconds, then relax.
  6. Move further into stretch, state the direction and recheck comfort.

Safety checks

  • Rule out hip pathology, fracture and recent surgery.
  • Do not stretch into sharp pain.
  • Stop for radiating leg pain or numbness.

Common errors

  • Forcing the stretch.
  • Not stabilizing the opposite hip.
  • Not telling the patient to relax.

Grading points

  • Position correct.
  • Muscle named.
  • Doctor contact named.
  • Stabilization stated.
  • Direction of stretch stated.
  • Patient informed and monitored.

T28 (Soft tissue): Set up a prone lumbar drop table soft tissue and light thrust for a hypertonic erector spinae region.

Patient position: Prone on a drop table with the pelvic piece correctly set.

Doctor position and stance: Standing at the side of the table with the shoulders over the contact.

Segment or listing: Right lumbar erector spinae hypertonicity with segmental restriction at L3.

Contact point: Contact on the right L3 mammillary process area. Doctor contact on the pisiform with the other hand reinforcing.

Line of drive: Posterior to anterior with a light, quick thrust timed with the drop of the table piece.

Setup steps

  1. Explain the table drop and ask for consent.
  2. Set the drop tension to the patient's weight.
  3. Position the patient prone on the piece.
  4. Palpate the muscle and the L3 level.
  5. Place the contact and take out the slack.
  6. State the direction and the timing of the drop.

Safety checks

  • Do not use with fracture, severe osteoporosis or cauda equina signs.
  • Use the lowest tension that works.
  • Stop if leg symptoms appear.

Common errors

  • Too much drop tension.
  • Contact on the spinous process only.
  • Not telling the patient about the drop.

Grading points

  • Table set up stated.
  • Position correct.
  • Contact named.
  • Doctor contact named.
  • Stabilization stated.
  • Line of drive and timing stated.

T29 (Soft tissue): Demonstrate a supine cervical traction and mobilization setup for neck stiffness without radiculopathy.

Patient position: Supine with the head supported on the table and the shoulders relaxed.

Doctor position and stance: Seated at the head of the table, facing the patient, with the forearms supported.

Segment or listing: General restriction of the lower cervical spine.

Contact point: Contact on the occiput and the upper neck. Doctor contact with the finger pads under the occiput.

Line of drive: Gentle long axis traction along the line of the spine with slow small oscillations, no thrust.

Setup steps

  1. Explain the technique and gain consent.
  2. Position the patient supine and relaxed.
  3. Place the finger pads under the occiput and rest the thumbs lightly on the face or the table.
  4. Take up the slack by a gentle pull along the spine.
  5. Add slow oscillations within comfort.
  6. State the direction and check for any symptoms.

Safety checks

  • Screen for arterial dysfunction, instability and recent trauma first.
  • Use light force.
  • Stop if dizziness, nausea or arm symptoms occur.

Common errors

  • Pulling with too much force.
  • Neck flexed or extended during traction.
  • Hands pressing on the front of the neck.

Grading points

  • Position correct.
  • Doctor position stated.
  • Contact named.
  • Hand placement stated.
  • Direction of traction stated.
  • Safety checks stated.

T30 (Soft tissue): Set up a side lying hip mobilization for a stiff hip joint using gentle traction.

Patient position: Side lying on the unaffected side with the affected hip and knee slightly flexed and supported by a pillow.

Doctor position and stance: Standing behind the patient at the hip, facing the patient's back.

Segment or listing: Right hip joint with restricted traction glide.

Contact point: Contact over the greater trochanter and the upper thigh. Doctor contact with both hands.

Line of drive: Gentle long axis traction along the femur followed by a slow, low amplitude oscillation.

Setup steps

  1. Tell the patient the plan and get consent.
  2. Position the patient side lying and the hip supported.
  3. Place one hand on the greater trochanter and the other on the thigh.
  4. Take up the slack with a gentle pull along the femur.
  5. Add slow oscillations as the patient tolerates.
  6. State the direction and recheck comfort.

Safety checks

  • Rule out hip fracture, avascular necrosis, infection and recent hip surgery.
  • Do not force the hip beyond a comfortable range.
  • Stop for groin pain, locking or numbness.

Common errors

  • Pulling too hard.
  • Wrong hand contact over the lateral thigh muscles only.
  • Patient not supported and not relaxed.

Grading points

  • Position correct.
  • Doctor position stated.
  • Contact named.
  • Hand placement stated.
  • Direction of traction stated.
  • Consent and safety stated.
